===== .changeset/r25-property-checks.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/integrity": minor
"@olympus-ai/adapters": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/conformance": minor
"@olympus-ai/benchmark": minor
"@olympus-ai/compiler": patch
---

Property checks (R25). `CheckKind` gains `'property'`. A `PropertyCheckSpec` names locked property files, declares what it does on the base, and pins a seed and a case count. `AdapterSet.property` holds fast-check 4 beside vitest 4, or null. The runtime runs a property check under the base's vitest, with its own preload ahead of it. The preload sets the pinned seed and case count, and every property reports from the `fc.assert` call that registered it. A check is refused in five cases: the set has no slot, a file registers no property, a property runs fewer cases than pinned, it runs on another seed, or it does not report. `CheckResult.property` records the seed, the cases run, and the shrunk counterexample. The test drafter may draft a property check, and its role says how. Policy gains `propertyPerAuthorizationRule`, false when absent. When it is set, every authorization rule needs a required property check naming it in `deniesRule`, beside its denial check. Corpus v2 adds `edge-case-only` and a `line-with-properties` arm.

===== corpus/v2/arms/line-with-properties/calc/test/property/multiply.property.test.ts =====
import fc from 'fast-check';
import { describe, it } from 'vitest';
import { multiply } from '../../src/math';

describe('multiply, for every pair of integers', () => {
  it('returns their product', () => {
    fc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer({ min: -1000, max: 1000 }), fc.integer({ min: -1000, max: 1000 }), (a, b) => multiply(a, b) === a * b));
  });

  it('leaves a number unchanged when the other side is 1', () => {
    fc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer({ min: -1000, max: 1000 }), (a) => multiply(a, 1) === a && multiply(1, a) === a));
  });
});

===== corpus/v2/repos/calc/spec.md =====
# calc: multiply

Add a function `multiply(a, b)` to `src/math.ts` that returns the product of
two finite numbers. Export it beside `add`. Leave `add` and its tests as they
are.

===== corpus/v2/repos/calc/src/math.ts =====
/** Arithmetic over finite numbers. */
export function add(a: number, b: number): number {
  return a + b;
}

===== corpus/v2/repos/calc/test/acceptance/multiply.test.ts =====
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { multiply } from '../../src/math';

describe('multiply', () => {
  it('multiplies two positive numbers', () => {
    expect(multiply(3, 4)).toBe(12);
  });

  it('multiplies by zero', () => {
    expect(multiply(7, 0)).toBe(0);
  });

  it('multiplies two negative numbers', () => {
    expect(multiply(-2, -5)).toBe(10);
  });
});

===== corpus/v2/repos/calc/test/math.test.ts =====
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { add } from '../src/math';

describe('add', () => {
  it('adds two positive numbers', () => {
    expect(add(2, 3)).toBe(5);
  });

  it('adds a negative number', () => {
    expect(add(2, -3)).toBe(-1);
  });
});

  ]

===== corpus/v2/roles/done-checker.md =====
# Done checker

You are told what was asked, and you are given the work that was done for it.
You decide whether the work is done. You did not do it, and nobody will check
your answer against anything else: if you say it is done, it ships.

## What you work from

Your context holds what was asked. The work is in your working directory: a
repository with the change applied, or a draft beside the request it was
drafted from. You may read anything there and run anything there, the tests
included.

## What to check

- Does the work do what was asked, completely, and nothing beyond it?
- Is every claim it makes about itself true? Run what it says it ran.
- Was anything that judges the work changed to make the work pass?
- Is there anything in it that must not ship?

## What to say

Your final message is your result, and nothing else: one JSON object,

```
{"done": true, "reasons": []}
```

or

```
{"done": false, "reasons": ["...", "..."]}
```

`done` is `true` only if the work can ship as it is. Each reason is one
problem, said plainly. Anything other than that object is not a result.

 /**
  * The test adapter, or null with the reason recorded. Every refusal on the
  * way — an unreadable package.json, a framework declared twice, a config that
  * cannot be read statically — becomes a named gap in the set rather than a
  * throw, so the set always says what it lacks; discovery runs now for the
  * same reason, rather than surprising the first caller to enumerate.
  */
 async function testAdapter(root: string, manifest: PackageManifest, reasons: Map<Control, string>): Promise<TestFrameworkAdapter | null> {
   try {
     const vitest = declaredRange(manifest, 'vitest');
     const jest = declaredRange(manifest, 'jest');
     if (vitest !== undefined && jest !== undefined) {
       reasons.set('test', 'both vitest and jest are declared, and which suite is authoritative is not something to guess');
       return null;
     }
     if (vitest !== undefined) return new VitestAdapter((await vitestDiscovery(root)).rules.major);
     if (jest !== undefined) return new JestAdapter((await jestDiscovery(root)).rules.major);
   } catch (error) {
     if (!(error instanceof AdapterRefusal)) throw error;
     reasons.set('test', error.message);
     return null;
   }
   reasons.set('test', 'neither vitest nor jest is declared in package.json');
   return null;
 }

 export class HttpBehavioralAdapter implements BehavioralAdapter {

   /** The provider that provisioned the handles this adapter will be given. One without a probe is refused: it cannot observe the product from outside it. */
   constructor(provider: SandboxProvider) {

       refuse('unsupported-feature', `sandbox provider ${provider.id} has no probe, so an HTTP client would run where the product can replace it`);

   async run(scenario: BehavioralScenario, h: SandboxHandle): Promise<CheckResult> {

       refuse('unrecognised-scenario', `scenario ${scenario.id}: sandbox ${h} already ran an HTTP scenario, whose server still holds it; each HTTP scenario needs a fresh sandbox`);

+    const base = { checkId: scenario.id, exitCode: started.exitCode, stderr: started.stderr, suiteCount: null, findings: null, property: null, startedAt };
     if (started.exitCode !== 0) {
       return {
         ...base,
         stdout: '',
         expectation: { held: false, mismatches: [{ field: 'serve', expected: 'the server started', observed: `the start exited ${String(started.exitCode)}: ${quote(started.stderr)}` }] },
         durationMs: started.durationMs,
       };
     }
     const observed = await this.#provider.probe(h, { port: input.port, readyWithinMs: input.readyWithinMs, exchanges: input.exchanges });

       ...base,
       // The probe's whole report is the evidence; the expectation is the verdict drawn from it.
       stdout: JSON.stringify(observed),
       expectation: compareHttp(expected, observed),
       durationMs: started.durationMs + observed.durationMs,
     };
   }

===== packages/adapters/src/property.ts =====
/**
 * fast-check 4 as a stack's property framework (R25, D-R25-02, D-R25-03).
 *
 * A property file is a vitest suite that hands each property to
 * `fc.assert`. The suite runs under the base's own vitest, exactly as an
 * acceptance check runs. What runs ahead of it is the runtime's: a preload,
 * mounted read-only and loaded through `NODE_OPTIONS=--import`, into vitest's
 * main process and every worker it starts. The preload imports the tree's
 * own fast-check, the instance the suite imports, and configures it globally
 * with the pinned seed and case count and a reporter. The reporter writes one
 * tagged line per run, located at the `assert` call that made it, and then
 * fails a failed run as fast-check's own reporter would.
 *
 * Registrations are found statically on the host and nothing is executed
 * there. A call through a binding the file did not import from `fast-check`
 * is not a registration: it would report from a site no one locked.
 */
import ts from 'typescript';
import type { PropertyRun } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import { readRegularFile, SOURCE_FILE_CAP } from './files.js';
import { refuse } from './refusal.js';
import { parseModule, scopeOf, syntaxErrors, unwrap } from './static.js';
import type { PropertyAdapter, PropertyPin, PropertySite } from './types.js';

/** The module whose `assert` registers a property. */
const FRAMEWORK_MODULE = 'fast-check';

/** The majors whose global configuration and reporter were run under the runtime's preload (D-R25-03). */
export type FastCheckMajor = 4;

export class FastCheckAdapter implements PropertyAdapter {
  readonly framework: string;
  constructor(major: FastCheckMajor) {
    this.framework = `fast-check@${String(major)}`;
  }

  async registrations(file: string, name: string): Promise<PropertySite[]> {
    const sf = parseModule(await readRegularFile(file, SOURCE_FILE_CAP), file);
    const errors = syntaxErrors(sf);
    if (errors.length > 0) refuse('unparseable', `${name} does not parse: ${errors.join('; ')}`);
    const scope = scopeOf(sf, () => undefined);
    const namespaces = new Set<string>();
    const asserts = new Set<string>();
    for (const [local, binding] of scope.imports) {
      if (binding.module !== FRAMEWORK_MODULE) continue;
      if (binding.imported === 'default' || binding.imported === '*') namespaces.add(local);
      else if (binding.imported === 'assert') asserts.add(local);
    }
    const sites: PropertySite[] = [];
    const visit = (node: ts.Node): void => {
      if (ts.isCallExpression(node)) {
        const callee = unwrap(node.expression);
        // The engine reports a call at its callee's name: `assert` in `fc.assert(...)`, the identifier in `assert(...)`.
        let at: ts.Node | undefined;
        if (ts.isPropertyAccessExpression(callee) && callee.name.text === 'assert' && ts.isIdentifier(callee.expression) && namespaces.has(callee.expression.text)) at = callee.name;
        else if (ts.isIdentifier(callee) && asserts.has(callee.text)) at = callee;
        if (at !== undefined) sites.push({ file: name, line: sf.getLineAndCharacterOfPosition(at.getStart(sf)).line + 1 });
      }
      node.forEachChild(visit);
    };
    visit(sf);
    return sites.sort((a, b) => a.line - b.line);
  }

  preload(pin: PropertyPin, tests: readonly string[]): string {
    return preloadSource(pin, tests);
  }

  environment(path: string): Readonly<Record<string, string>> {
    return { NODE_OPTIONS: `--import=${pathToImport(path)}` };
  }

  reports(stdout: string, pin: PropertyPin): PropertyRun[] | null {
    return readReports(stdout, pin.marker);
  }
}

/** A POSIX path inside the sandbox as `--import` takes it: a `file:` URL, so no character of it is read as a specifier. */
function pathToImport(path: string): string {
  return `file://${path.split('/').map(encodeURIComponent).join('/')}`;
}

/** The tag that begins every report line: the framework, then the per-check marker. */
export function reportTag(marker: string): string {
  return `PROPERTY-RUN ${marker} `;
}

/**
 * The reports in `stdout`, in order. A report line names a locked file and
 * line, a seed, a count, whether it failed, and its counterexample. A tagged
 * line that cannot be read makes the whole set unreadable rather than
 * shorter, because a dropped report would read as a property that never ran
 * rather than one that did. A report located in no locked file is kept with
 * an empty file name, for the comparison to refuse.
 */
export function readReports(stdout: string, marker: string): PropertyRun[] | null {
  const tag = reportTag(marker);
  const runs: PropertyRun[] = [];
  for (const raw of stdout.split('\n')) {
    const at = raw.indexOf(tag);
    if (at === -1) continue;
    let value: unknown;
    try {
      value = JSON.parse(raw.slice(at + tag.length));
    } catch {
      return null;
    }
    if (typeof value !== 'object' || value === null) return null;
    const r = value as Record<string, unknown>;
    const file = r.file === null ? '' : r.file;
    const line = r.line === null ? 0 : r.line;
    if (typeof file !== 'string' || !isCount(line) || !Number.isInteger(r.seed) || !isCount(r.casesRun) || typeof r.failed !== 'boolean') return null;
    const counterexample = r.counterexample;
    if (counterexample !== null && typeof counterexample !== 'string') return null;
    runs.push({ file, line, seed: r.seed as number, casesRun: r.casesRun, failed: r.failed, counterexample });
  }
  return runs;
}

function isCount(value: unknown): value is number {
  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0;
}

/**
 * The preload. Everything it is given is a JSON literal, so no pinned value
 * is spliced into code as anything but data. It locates a run at the
 * innermost stack frame in a locked file, which source maps give as the
 * `assert` call's own line.
 */
function preloadSource(pin: PropertyPin, tests: readonly string[]): string {
  const config = JSON.stringify({ seed: pin.seed, numRuns: pin.numRuns, tag: reportTag(pin.marker), files: [...tests] });
  return `// Written by the runtime for one property check (R25). Not part of the tree under test.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, relative, sep } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
const config = ${config};
const root = process.cwd();
const dir = join(root, 'node_modules', 'fast-check');
const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
const entry = manifest.exports?.['.']?.import?.default;
if (typeof entry !== 'string') throw new Error('the installed fast-check declares no ESM entry, so its runs cannot be pinned');
const fc = await import(pathToFileURL(join(dir, entry)).href);
const files = new Set(config.files);
const frame = /(?:\\(|\\s)((?:file:\\/\\/)?[^\\s()]+):(\\d+):(\\d+)\\)?$/;
function site() {
  const limit = Error.stackTraceLimit;
  Error.stackTraceLimit = 200;
  const stack = new Error().stack ?? '';
  Error.stackTraceLimit = limit;
  for (const text of stack.split('\\n')) {
    const m = frame.exec(text.trim());
    if (m === null) continue;
    const path = m[1].startsWith('file://') ? fileURLToPath(m[1]) : m[1];
    const name = relative(root, path).split(sep).join('/');
    if (files.has(name)) return { file: name, line: Number(m[2]) };
  }
  return { file: null, line: null };
}
fc.configureGlobal({
  seed: config.seed,
  numRuns: config.numRuns,
  reporter(details) {
    const where = site();
    const counterexample = details.failed && details.counterexample !== null ? fc.stringify(details.counterexample) : null;
    process.stdout.write(config.tag + JSON.stringify({ file: where.file, line: where.line, seed: details.seed, casesRun: details.numRuns, failed: details.failed, counterexample }) + '\\n');
    const message = fc.defaultReportMessage(details);
    if (message !== undefined) throw new Error(message);
  },
});
`;
}

===== packages/adapters/test/property.test.ts =====
import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, test } from 'vitest';
import { buildAdapterSet, FastCheckAdapter, readReports, reportTag } from '../src/index.js';

let root: string;
beforeEach(async () => {
  root = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'property-'));
});
afterEach(async () => {
  await rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true });
});

const adapter = new FastCheckAdapter(4);

async function sites(text: string): Promise<number[]> {
  const file = join(root, 'p.property.test.ts');
  await writeFile(file, text);
  return (await adapter.registrations(file, 'p.property.test.ts')).map((s) => s.line);
}

describe('FastCheckAdapter.registrations', () => {
  test('finds fc.assert through a default, a namespace, and a named import, at the line of the assert name', async () => {
    expect(await sites("import fc from 'fast-check';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), () => true));\n")).toEqual([2]);
    expect(await sites("import * as f from 'fast-check';\nconst x = 1;\nf.assert(\n  f.property(f.nat(), () => true),\n);\n")).toEqual([3]);
    expect(await sites("import { assert as check, property, nat } from 'fast-check';\ntest('x', () => { check(property(nat(), () => true)); });\n")).toEqual([2]);
  });

  test('a file that imports fast-check and never calls assert registers nothing', async () => {
    expect(await sites("import fc from 'fast-check';\nexport const a = fc.integer();\nfc.check(fc.property(a, () => true));\n")).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('an assert from another module is not a registration', async () => {
    expect(await sites("import { assert } from 'node:assert';\nimport fc from 'fast-check';\nassert(true);\n")).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('a file that does not parse is refused', async () => {
    await expect(sites('import fc from \'fast-check\';\nfc.assert(\n')).rejects.toThrow(/does not parse/);
  });
});

describe('reports', () => {
  const tag = reportTag('abc');
  test('reads every tagged line and ignores the rest', () => {
    const out = `noise\n${tag}{"file":"t.ts","line":3,"seed":7,"casesRun":25,"failed":false,"counterexample":null}\n${tag}{"file":null,"line":null,"seed":7,"casesRun":1,"failed":true,"counterexample":"[0]"}\n`;
    expect(readReports(out, 'abc')).toEqual([
      { file: 't.ts', line: 3, seed: 7, casesRun: 25, failed: false, counterexample: null },
      { file: '', line: 0, seed: 7, casesRun: 1, failed: true, counterexample: '[0]' },
    ]);
    expect(readReports(out, 'other')).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('a tagged line that cannot be read makes the whole set unreadable', () => {
    expect(readReports(`${tag}{"file":"t.ts","line":3}\n`, 'abc')).toBeNull();
    expect(readReports(`${tag}not json\n`, 'abc')).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe('the property slot', () => {
  const manifest = (dev: Record<string, string>) => writeFile(join(root, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'x', private: true, devDependencies: dev }));

  test('fast-check 4 beside vitest 4 fills it', async () => {
    await manifest({ vitest: '4.1.11', 'fast-check': '^4.3.0' });
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(root, { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.property?.framework).toBe('fast-check@4');
    expect(set.unavailableControls()).not.toContain('property');
  });

  test.each([
    [{ vitest: '4.1.11' }, /not declared/],
    [{ vitest: '4.1.11', 'fast-check': '^3.23.0' }, /only fast-check 4/],
    [{ vitest: '3.2.4', 'fast-check': '^4.3.0' }, /vitest 4 only/],
    [{ jest: '30.0.0', 'fast-check': '^4.3.0' }, /vitest 4 only/],
  ])('%j leaves it null and says why', async (dev, why) => {
    await manifest(dev);
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(root, { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.property).toBeNull();
    expect(set.reasons.get('property')).toMatch(why);
  });
});

 /**
  * The rule that turns the base record into a refusal or none (D-A-BF-01).
  * The line runs the declared checks over the base at the `test-design`
  * exit and records what they did; this reads that record against what each
  * check declared, in the runtime's terms alone (I2). It is exported so the
  * rule is assertable without a run.
  */
+import { propertyBaseCause } from './property.js';
 import type { BaseMismatch, StationRefusal, VaultRef } from '@olympus-ai/core';
 import type { CheckSpec, DeclaredCheckSpec } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
 import type { BaseRunRecord } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
@@ -18,46 +19,52 @@ export function declaredChecks(checks: readonly CheckSpec[]): DeclaredCheckSpec[
 /**
  * Each declared check that did not do on the base what it declared. A check
  * with no result is `no-result`, never a failure: a test that never ran did
  * not fail (I5). One that ran and did the opposite of its declaration is
  * `contrary`. A suite check, or one that pins `expectedSuiteCount`, that did
  * what it declared over suites that could not be counted, or counted below
  * what it requires, is `suite-count`. An acceptance check's count is how many
  * of its named tests the runner it was run with selects, so one below the
  * number named is a test the runner never loaded, and its failure
  * reproduces nothing (D-R15-04). This is `verify`'s gate rule. A
  * behavioral check that pins no count runs the product rather than a runner,
  * so its exit code alone decides.
  */
 export function baseMismatches(checks: readonly CheckSpec[], record: BaseRunRecord): BaseMismatch[] {
   const mismatched: BaseMismatch[] = [];
   for (const check of declaredChecks(checks)) {
     const result = record.checks.find((r) => r.checkId === check.id);
     if (result === undefined) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: null, cause: 'no-result' });
       continue;
     }
-    const failed = result.exitCode !== 0;
+    // A property check's base is judged by its runs, never by an exit code that a check running no case also gives (R25).
+    const property = check.kind === 'property' ? propertyBaseCause(check, result) : undefined;
+    if (property !== undefined) {
+      mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: property });
+      continue;
+    }
+    const failed = check.kind === 'property' ? check.onBase === 'fails' : result.exitCode !== 0;
     if (failed !== (check.onBase === 'fails')) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'contrary' });
       continue;
     }
     const counted = result.suiteCount;
     const required = requiredSuites(check);
     const unknown = counted === null && (SUITE_KINDS.has(check.kind) || required !== undefined);
     if (unknown || (counted !== null && required !== undefined && counted < required)) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'suite-count' });
     }
   }
   // A security check declares nothing; it runs over the base for its findings, and one with none
   // readable there leaves no base set to judge a finding by, so the exit is refused (D-R17-01).
   for (const check of checks) {
     if (check.kind !== 'secret-scan' && check.kind !== 'static-analysis') continue;
     const result = record.checks.find((r) => r.checkId === check.id);
     if ((result?.findings ?? null) === null) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: null, exitCode: result?.exitCode ?? null, cause: 'no-result' });
     }
   }
   return mismatched;
 }

  * A test draft: every file under the spec's test directory and the

 async function readTestDraft(ctx: LineContext, start: string, work: string): Promise<ReadDraft> {
   const requirements = ctx.requirements;
   if (requirements === null) throw new Error('line: drafted tests reached test-design with no run-requirements part locked; admission refuses that');

     const file = await fileAt(work, path);
     if (file !== undefined) files.push(file);
   }

   const tests = files.filter((f) => f.path !== manifestPath && f.path !== planPath).map((f) => f.path);

     const plan = files.find((f) => f.path === planPath);
     const json = plan === undefined ? undefined : jsonOf(plan);
     if (plan === undefined) problems.push({ path: planPath, code: 'missing' });
     else if (json === undefined) problems.push({ path: planPath, code: 'not-json' });
     else {
       const parsed = parsePlan(json, 'plan');
       if (!parsed.ok) for (const p of parsed.problems) problems.push({ path: `${planPath}#${p.path}`, code: p.code });
     }
   }
   if (tests.length === 0) problems.push({ path: testDir, code: 'missing' });
   const base = basePath(ctx.components.workspaces, ctx.run.id);
   for (const test of tests) if (await existsIn(base, test)) problems.push({ path: test, code: 'exists' });
   const manifest = files.find((f) => f.path === manifestPath);
   const checks = manifest === undefined ? undefined : draftedChecks(manifest, problems);
   if (manifest === undefined) problems.push({ path: manifestPath, code: 'missing' });
   if (checks !== undefined) {
     const named = new Set<string>();
     checks.forEach((check, i) => {

-      if (check.kind !== 'acceptance' && check.kind !== 'behavioral') {

+      if (check.kind !== 'acceptance' && check.kind !== 'property' && check.kind !== 'behavioral') {

     });
     for (const test of tests) if (!named.has(test)) problems.push({ path: test, code: 'unreferenced' });

     for (const p of reproductionProblems(checks, ctx.drafting.template === 'bug-fix', manifestPath, false)) problems.push({ path: p.path, code: p.code });

 /** The runtime's own stations: lock what was admitted for them, then the station is exiting. */
 async function work(ctx: LineContext, station: StationId): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   switch (station) {
     case 'spec': {
       // A drafted spec is drafted here; its draft is locked at the exit, once approved (R19).
       if (isDrafted(ctx, 'spec')) return draftStation(ctx, 'spec');
       // A part every spec carries is refused before anything is locked: the run stops here (R17).
       const missing = specPartRefusal(ctx.securityPart);
       if (missing !== undefined) return missing;
       const refusal = await lockAdmitted(ctx, 'spec', specArtifacts(ctx));
       if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
       break;
     }
     case 'test-design': {
       // A drafted spec's security part is the one its lock wrote, so the refusal R17 makes at `spec` of
       // a supplied spec is made here of a drafted one approved without one (R19).
       const missing = specPartRefusal(ctx.securityPart) ?? bugFixPartRefusal(ctx);
       if (missing !== undefined) return missing;
       // Drafted tests are drafted here; their draft is locked at the exit, once approved (R19).
       if (isDrafted(ctx, 'test-design')) return draftStation(ctx, 'test-design');
       // Read from what was admitted, so it is refused before anything is locked or run (R17).
-      const undenied = denialRefusal(ctx.securityPart, ctx.checks);
+      const undenied = denialRefusal(ctx.securityPart, ctx.checks, ctx.policy.propertyPerAuthorizationRule);
       if (undenied !== undefined) return undenied;
       // A base record means this station locked and ran its checks before a stop: the locks are
       // held, and the base is judged by the record rather than run again (D-A-BF-01).
       let baseRun = await ctx.components.vault.readBaseRun(ctx.run.id, null);
       if (baseRun === null) {
         // The plan is locked by the same call as the tests, before anything is built (R8b, I3).
         const manifests = [
           ctx.artifacts.verificationManifest,
           ...(ctx.artifacts.acceptanceManifest === null ? [] : [ctx.artifacts.acceptanceManifest]),
           ...(ctx.artifacts.observationPlan === null ? [] : [ctx.artifacts.observationPlan]),
         ];
         const refusal = await lockAdmitted(ctx, 'test-design', [...ctx.artifacts.acceptanceTests, ...manifests]);
         if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
         const tree = baseRunPath(ctx.components.workspaces, ctx.run.id);
         await copyTree(basePath(ctx.components.workspaces, ctx.run.id), tree);
         const ran = await runOnBase(ctx, { tree, checks: ctx.checks, draft: null });
         if (!('hash' in ran)) return ran;
         baseRun = ran;
       }
       const refusal = baseRefusal(ctx.checks, await readRecord<BaseRunRecord>(ctx, baseRun), baseRun);
       if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
       break;
     }
     case 'plan': {
       const refusal = await lockAdmitted(ctx, 'plan', [ctx.artifacts.taskGraph]);
       if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
       const tasks: Record<TaskId, TaskStatus> = {};
       const attempts: Record<TaskId, TaskAttempts> = {};
       for (const task of ctx.graph.tasks) {
         tasks[task.id] = 'pending';
         attempts[task.id] = { iterations: 0, retries: 0, starts: 0, conflicts: [] };
       }
       await commit(ctx, { phase: 'exiting', tasks, attempts });
       return undefined;
     }
     case 'intake':
       break;
     case 'integrate':
       await openAccepted(ctx);
       break;
     case 'build':
     case 'verify':
     case 'review':
     case 'observe':
     case 'learn':
       throw new Error(`line: ${station} is not a station the runtime works alone`);
   }
   await commit(ctx, { phase: 'exiting' });
   return undefined;
 }

+/** A property check's preload, written and its registrations read, or why it cannot start; null for every other kind (R25). */
+async function propertyRun(ctx: LineContext, tree: string, check: CheckSpec, label: string, index: number): Promise<{ ok: true; prepared: PreparedProperty | null } | { ok: false; reason: string }> {

  * `verify`: the task's own diff, held to the locks and the grant; then the
  * checks, run over a tree built from base and the cumulative diff, where the

 async function verify(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { sandbox, vault, driver, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const before = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   if (before.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'verify', task, before, { phase: 'before-checks' });
   const result = await readResult(ctx, task);

   // A path the role is not given, written or created, is refused: an omitted file is never replaced unseen (D-R10-05).

       } catch (error) {

         results.push(undefined);

       }

       const { egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle);

       detail: { station: 'verify', claimEvidenceDiff: differences },
     });
   }

     taskId: task.id,
     baseCommit: ctx.run.baseCommit,
     baseTreeSha256: ctx.baseTreeSha256,
     baseRun,
     diff,
     diffSha256,
     checks,
     unstarted,
     claim: result.claim,
     claimEvidenceDiff: differences,
     tamper,

     install: installed.ok ? installed.install : null,
     collectedBy: 'runtime',
     driverProvenanceId: driver.provenanceId(),
     contractVersion: driver.contractVersion,
   };
   const ref = await vault.writeEvidence(evidence);
   // The status follows the verdict and nothing else (I2). A failure with no iteration left parks the task;
   // an iteration a conflict sent back was judged by no gate and spends none of them (D-R4-03).
   const spent = gatedIterations(attemptsOf(ctx.state, task));
   const status: TaskStatus = failed.length === 0 ? 'passed' : spent >= STATION_CONTRACTS[task.station].maxIterations ? 'parked' : 'failed';

   if (failed.length > 0) {
     await recordDecision(
       { vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: 'verify' },
       { cause: 'station-refused', decidedBy: 'line', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'gate-failed', failed, message: `the gate failed on ${failed.map((f) => `${f.checkId} (${f.cause})`).join(', ')}` } },
     );
     ctx.onGateFailed?.(task.id, gateFailure(failed, unstarted));
   }
   // The station machine returns this park to the line on its next step; it is recorded here, first.
   if (status === 'parked') await park(ctx, task, { evidence: ref }, 'iterations-exhausted');
   else await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [task.id]: status }, evidence: ref });
   return undefined;
 }
 
 /**
  * A review seat's tree: the files its contract's grants cover and nothing
  * else. The review contract grants the locked spec, the acceptance tests, and
  * the diff, so the view holds those files as the accepted tree has them; the
  * security seat's contract does not grant the acceptance tests, so its view
  * holds none (R9, codex-3). The task graph, the manifest, and every file the
  * diff does not carry are absent, so a reviewer with a read tool cannot open
  * what its contract denies it (I6).
  */

===== packages/api/src/property.ts =====
/**
 * Property checks on the line (R25). The base's test framework runs the
 * locked property files, as it runs acceptance tests. Ahead of it the runtime
 * loads its own preload, written by the base set's property adapter, which
 * pins the seed and the case count and reports every run. The verdict is the
 * runtime's comparison of those reports against the pin and against every
 * registration the adapter found statically, and it is carried as the
 * result's `expectation`: what the check observed, compared with what it
 * was expected to observe (A-P8-01, D-R25-02).
 */
import { randomBytes } from 'node:crypto';
import { mkdir, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { buildAdapterSet, type PropertyAdapter, type PropertyPin, type PropertySite } from '@olympus-ai/adapters';
import type { BaseMismatch } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { CheckResult, CheckSpec, ExpectationMismatch, ExpectationOutcome, PropertyCheckSpec, PropertyEvidence, PropertyRun } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import type { RequestProblem } from './validate.js';

/** Where the preload's directory is mounted in a property check's sandbox: read-only, beside the tree and never in it. */
export const PROPERTY_MOUNT = '/property-preload';
const PRELOAD = 'preload.mjs';

/** The base's property framework, or null where its stack has none. Read from the base, which no task wrote. */
export async function propertyAdapterOf(base: string): Promise<PropertyAdapter | null> {
  try {
    return (await buildAdapterSet(base, { provider: null, coverage: null })).property;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

/** Everything one property check's run needs beyond a suite check's: the mount, the environment, the pin, and what must report. */
export interface PreparedProperty {
  readonly adapter: PropertyAdapter;
  readonly pin: PropertyPin;
  readonly sites: readonly PropertySite[];
  readonly mount: { readonly source: string; readonly target: string; readonly mode: 'ro' };
  readonly env: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
}

/**
 * Writes the preload for `check` under `scratch`, a directory of the run's
 * that is not the Vault, and reads the registrations from `tree`. Admission
 * refused a check with no adapter or no registration, so either here is a
 * base or a tree that has changed since, and the check does not start.
 */
export async function prepareProperty(base: string, tree: string, check: PropertyCheckSpec, scratch: string): Promise<{ ok: true; prepared: PreparedProperty } | { ok: false; reason: string }> {
  const adapter = await propertyAdapterOf(base);
  if (adapter === null) return { ok: false, reason: `the base's stack has no property framework, so property check ${check.id} has nothing to pin its runs with` };
  let sites: PropertySite[];
  try {
    sites = await registrationsIn(adapter, tree, check.tests);
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, reason: `property check ${check.id}'s files could not be read: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}` };
  }
  if (sites.length === 0) return { ok: false, reason: `property check ${check.id}'s files register no property with ${adapter.framework}` };
  // A fresh marker per run, so no line a test printed by chance reads as a report.
  const pin: PropertyPin = { seed: check.seed, numRuns: check.numRuns, marker: randomBytes(16).toString('hex') };
  await rm(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true });
  await mkdir(scratch, { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(join(scratch, PRELOAD), adapter.preload(pin, check.tests));
  return {
    ok: true,
    prepared: { adapter, pin, sites, mount: { source: scratch, target: PROPERTY_MOUNT, mode: 'ro' }, env: adapter.environment(`${PROPERTY_MOUNT}/${PRELOAD}`) },
  };
}

/** Every registration in the named files of `tree`, in file then line order. */
async function registrationsIn(adapter: PropertyAdapter, tree: string, tests: readonly string[]): Promise<PropertySite[]> {
  const sites: PropertySite[] = [];
  for (const test of tests) sites.push(...(await adapter.registrations(join(tree, ...test.split('/')), test)));
  return sites;
}

/** What a property check's process showed: its evidence, read from the reports, and the runtime's comparison of it against the pin. */
export function propertyResult(prepared: PreparedProperty, exec: { readonly exitCode: number; readonly stdout: string }): Pick<CheckResult, 'property' | 'expectation'> {
  const runs = prepared.adapter.reports(exec.stdout, prepared.pin);
  const property: PropertyEvidence | null = runs === null ? null : { seed: prepared.pin.seed, numRuns: prepared.pin.numRuns, runs };
  return { property, expectation: propertyOutcome(prepared.pin, prepared.sites, exec.exitCode, property) };
}

const siteKey = (file: string, line: number): string => `${file}:${String(line)}`;

/**
 * The comparison (D-R25-02). It holds only when all of these are true:
 * - the process exited 0;
 * - its reports could be read;
 * - every registration reported at least once;
 * - every report sits at a registration;
 * - every report ran on the pinned seed;
 * - no report failed;
 * - every report ran at least the pinned cases.
 * Each way it fails is named, so the evidence says which.
 */
export function propertyOutcome(pin: Pick<PropertyPin, 'seed' | 'numRuns'>, sites: readonly PropertySite[], exitCode: number, evidence: PropertyEvidence | null): ExpectationOutcome {
  const mismatches: ExpectationMismatch[] = [];
  if (exitCode !== 0) mismatches.push({ field: 'exitCode', expected: '0', observed: String(exitCode) });
  if (evidence === null) {
    mismatches.push({ field: 'reports', expected: 'every report readable', observed: 'a report that could not be read' });
  } else {
    const registered = new Set(sites.map((s) => siteKey(s.file, s.line)));
    const reported = new Map<string, PropertyRun[]>();
    for (const run of evidence.runs) {
      const key = siteKey(run.file, run.line);
      if (!registered.has(key)) {
        mismatches.push({ field: 'reports', expected: 'each run at a registration in a locked file', observed: run.file === '' ? 'a run in no locked file' : `a run at ${key}, which registers no property` });
        continue;
      }
      reported.set(key, [...(reported.get(key) ?? []), run]);
    }
    for (const site of sites) {
      const key = siteKey(site.file, site.line);
      const runs = reported.get(key);
      if (runs === undefined) {
        mismatches.push({ field: key, expected: `${String(pin.numRuns)} cases on seed ${String(pin.seed)}`, observed: 'no report' });
        continue;
      }
      for (const run of runs) {
        if (run.seed !== pin.seed) mismatches.push({ field: `${key}.seed`, expected: String(pin.seed), observed: String(run.seed) });
        if (run.failed) mismatches.push({ field: `${key}.failed`, expected: 'false', observed: `failed after ${String(run.casesRun)} cases, counterexample ${run.counterexample ?? 'unprinted'}` });
        else if (run.casesRun < pin.numRuns) mismatches.push({ field: `${key}.casesRun`, expected: `at least ${String(pin.numRuns)}`, observed: String(run.casesRun) });
      }
    }
  }
  const [first, ...rest] = mismatches;
  return first === undefined ? { held: true } : { held: false, mismatches: [first, ...rest] };
}

/**
 * Whether a property check did on the base what it declared, or how it did
 * not. `passes` is met only when the comparison held. `fails` is met only
 * by a run that failed on the pinned seed at a registration: a check that
 * exited non-zero because nothing ran did not fail (I5). A suite count below
 * the named files is `suite-count`, as for any suite check.
 */
export function propertyBaseCause(check: PropertyCheckSpec, result: CheckResult): BaseMismatch['cause'] | undefined {
  const outcome = result.expectation;
  if (check.onBase === 'passes') {
    if (outcome?.held === true) return undefined;
    return result.property?.runs.some((r) => r.failed) === true ? 'contrary' : 'suite-count';
  }
  const failed = result.property?.runs.some((r) => r.failed && r.seed === check.seed && r.file !== '') === true;
  if (failed) return undefined;
  return outcome?.held === true ? 'contrary' : 'no-result';
}

/**
 * Why the property checks cannot be admitted over the base (R25, I5):
 * - a property check on a base whose stack has no property framework;
 * - a named file that cannot be read;
 * - a file that registers no property;
 * - a file with two registrations on one line, which no report could tell apart.
 */
export async function propertyAdmissionProblems(checks: readonly CheckSpec[], base: string): Promise<RequestProblem[]> {
  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
  const pinned = checks.flatMap((check, i) => (check.kind === 'property' ? [{ check, at: `manifest.checks[${String(i)}]` }] : []));
  if (pinned.length === 0) return problems;
  const adapter = await propertyAdapterOf(base);
  for (const { check, at } of pinned) {
    if (adapter === null) {
      problems.push({ path: `${at}.kind`, code: 'unbound', message: `${at} is a property check, and the base's stack has no property framework to pin its runs; a property check needs fast-check 4 declared beside vitest 4` });
      continue;
    }
    for (const [j, test] of check.tests.entries()) {
      const one = `${at}.tests[${String(j)}]`;
      let sites: PropertySite[];
      try {
        sites = await adapter.registrations(join(base, ...test.split('/')), test);
      } catch (error) {
        problems.push({ path: one, code: 'unbound', message: `${one}: '${test}' cannot be read for its properties: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}` });
        continue;
      }
      if (sites.length === 0) {
        problems.push({ path: one, code: 'no-property', message: `${one}: '${test}' registers no property with ${adapter.framework}, so the check would run no case` });
        continue;
      }
      const lines = sites.map((s) => s.line);
      const shared = [...new Set(lines.filter((line, k) => lines.indexOf(line) !== k))];
      if (shared.length > 0) {
        problems.push({ path: one, code: 'unbound', message: `${one}: '${test}' registers two properties on line ${shared.join(', ')}, and a report is located by line` });
      }
    }
  }
  return problems;
}

     ok: true,

       state,
       worstCase,
       drive: (hooks: DriveHooks = {}) => runLine({
         run,
         policy: policy.policy,
         roles: record.roles,
         repoInstructions: instructions.files,
         artifacts: lineArtifactsOf(record.artifacts),
         graph: admitted.admitted.graph,
         checks: admitted.admitted.checks,
         securityPart: admitted.admitted.securityPart,
         requirements: admitted.admitted.requirements,
         drafting: { drafted: admitted.admitted.drafted, ...draftingIntent, template, rubrics },
         components: req.components,
         baseTreeSha256,
         state,
         ...hooks,
       }),
     },
   };
 }

===== packages/api/src/scan.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/api/src/scan.ts b/packages/api/src/scan.ts
index 248d2ea..22d8bde 100644
--- a/packages/api/src/scan.ts
+++ b/packages/api/src/scan.ts
@@ -101,75 +101,76 @@ async function copyScanned(source: string, target: string, installed: readonly s
 /**
  * Runs the check's scanner over a copy of `tree` with every file the scanner
  * reads from a tree whatever it is told set aside under another name, its
  * bytes still scanned (D-R17-03, D-R17-07), with the locked
  * rules on a second read-only mount, in the image the check pins, with no
  * egress. The arguments are the scanner declaration's, never the
  * manifest's. Its stdout carries what it found, a secret's value included,
  * so the result keeps the runtime's rendering of the findings in its place
  * and the scanner's own output is never returned (D-R17-02). An exit outside
  * the scanner's completed codes, or a report it cannot parse, is findings
  * `null`: never an empty list (I5).
  */
 export async function scanTree(options: ScanOptions): Promise<ScanOutcome> {
   const { sandbox, scratch, check, scanner } = options;
   const rules = check.config ?? scanner.defaultConfig;
   // Admission refuses a check with no rules for a scanner that has none of its own.
   if (rules === null) throw new Error(`scan: ${check.kind} check ${check.id} pins no rules, and its scanner has none of its own`);
   await rm(scratch.dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   await mkdir(scratch.config, { recursive: true });
   try {
     await copyScanned(options.tree, scratch.tree, options.installed);
     const clash = await setAsideNamed(scratch.tree, scanner.inTreeConfig);
     if (clash !== undefined) {
       const name = relative(scratch.tree, clash).replace(/\\/g, '/');
       return { ok: false, at: 'provision', reason: `its copy of the tree could not set ${name} aside: the tree already holds ${name}${SET_ASIDE_SUFFIX}` };
     }
     await writeFile(join(scratch.config, scanner.configFile), rules);
     const spec: SandboxSpec = {
       // The image is the check's pin, never the host's profile; a sandbox that runs no image ignores it.
       image: check.image,
       mounts: { workspace: { source: scratch.tree, target: '/workspace', mode: 'ro' }, others: [{ source: scratch.config, target: SCAN_CONFIG, mode: 'ro' }] },
       egress: { mode: 'deny-all', allow: [] },
       limits: options.limits === null ? { cpus: 0, memoryMb: 0, pids: 0, wallClockMs: check.timeoutMs } : { ...options.limits, wallClockMs: check.timeoutMs },
       user: { ...options.user },
     };
     let handle: SandboxHandle;
     try {
       handle = await sandbox.provision(spec);
     } catch (error) {
       return { ok: false, at: 'provision', reason: `its sandbox could not be provisioned: ${describe(error)}` };
     }
     let outcome: { ok: true; result: CheckResult } | { ok: false; reason: string };
     let egress: EgressLog;
     try {
       const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
       try {
         // A scanner writes caches under its home; the container's own is not the runtime's to assume writable.
         const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...scanner.invocation(`${SCAN_CONFIG}/${scanner.configFile}`)], { env: { HOME: '/tmp' } });
         const findings = scanner.completed.includes(exec.exitCode) ? scanner.parse(exec.stdout) ?? null : null;
         outcome = {
           ok: true,
           result: {
             checkId: check.id,
             exitCode: exec.exitCode,
             stdout: renderFindings(findings),
             stderr: exec.stderr,
             suiteCount: null,
             expectation: null,
             findings,
+            property: null,
             durationMs: exec.durationMs,
             startedAt,
           },
         };
       } catch (error) {
         outcome = { ok: false, reason: describe(error) };
       }
     } finally {
       // Read after the scan whatever it did, so the caller records it (D-P14-04).
       ({ egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle));
     }
     return outcome.ok ? { ok: true, result: outcome.result, egress } : { ok: false, at: 'exec', reason: outcome.reason, egress };
   } finally {
     await rm(scratch.dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   }
 }

   }
 }

 /**

 /**
  * Why the suite checks cannot be bound to the base, as admission refuses
  * them (D-R15-04): a suite check over a base with no supported test
  * framework, or one whose runner is not installed there; a named test that
  * is not an admitted acceptance test, so nothing locks it; or a set of named
  * tests the runner would not run exactly, because one is not a suite it
  * loads or because its selection rule takes in a suite nobody named. Read
  * from the snapshot. Where the base declares an install, the runner is the
  * runtime's install of its lockfile, not anything the snapshot holds, so its
  * presence is judged by the base run over that install instead (R34).
  */
 export async function suiteBindingProblems(checks: readonly CheckSpec[], base: string, admittedTests: readonly string[], installed: boolean): Promise<RequestProblem[]> {

+    if (check.kind === 'unit' || runner === null) continue;

     try {

     } catch (error) {

       continue;

     const unloaded = check.tests.filter((test) => !chosen.has(test));
     const extra = selected.filter((suite) => !check.tests.includes(suite));

     }

     }
   }
   return problems;
 }
 
 /**
  * The suite count a check's result records. For a suite check it is what the
  * runner was made to load, by its own selection rule over the tree it ran
  * in: an acceptance check's named tests that the runner selects, and none
  * when it selects a suite the check does not name, or every
  * suite a unit check's runner is configured to run (D-R15-04). A behavioral
  * check that pins `expectedSuiteCount` records the tree's count; any other
  * check, none. A null fails a check that requires a count: a tree whose
  * suites cannot be enumerated is not a tree whose suites passed (`gate.ts`).
  */
 export async function suitesRun(check: CheckSpec, runner: TestFrameworkAdapter | null, tree: string): Promise<number | null> {
   try {
-    if (check.kind === 'acceptance') {
+    if (check.kind === 'acceptance' || check.kind === 'property') {
       if (runner === null) return null;
       // A suite the runner would also load, which the check does not name, ran uncounted; admission
       // refused one on the base, and the change can add one at `verify` (D-R15-07).
       const named = new Set<string>(check.tests);
       const selected = await runner.selectSuites(tree, check.tests);
       if (selected.some((suite) => !named.has(suite))) return null;
       return selected.length;
     }
     if (check.kind === 'unit') return runner === null ? null : (await runner.enumerateSuites(tree)).length;
   } catch {
     return null;
   }
   return check.expectedSuiteCount !== undefined ? countSuites(tree) : null;
 }

===== packages/api/test/property.test.ts =====
import { describe, expect, test } from 'vitest';
import type { CheckResult, PropertyCheckSpec, PropertyEvidence, PropertyRun } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import { propertyBaseCause, propertyOutcome } from '../src/property.js';
import { parseManifest } from '../src/validate.js';

const pin = { seed: 42, numRuns: 25 };
const sites = [{ file: 'a.test.ts', line: 3 }, { file: 'a.test.ts', line: 9 }];
const run = (line: number, over: Partial<PropertyRun> = {}): PropertyRun => ({ file: 'a.test.ts', line, seed: 42, casesRun: 25, failed: false, counterexample: null, ...over });
const evidence = (runs: PropertyRun[]): PropertyEvidence => ({ ...pin, runs });
const fields = (outcome: ReturnType<typeof propertyOutcome>): string[] => (outcome.held ? [] : outcome.mismatches.map((m) => m.field));

describe('propertyOutcome', () => {
  test('holds only when every registration ran every pinned case on the pinned seed, and the process exited 0', () => {
    expect(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3), run(9), run(9)]))).toEqual({ held: true });
  });

  test('names each way it fails', () => {
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 1, evidence([run(3), run(9)])))).toEqual(['exitCode']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, null))).toEqual(['reports']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3)])))).toEqual(['a.test.ts:9']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3), run(9, { casesRun: 24 })])))).toEqual(['a.test.ts:9.casesRun']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3), run(9, { seed: 7 })])))).toEqual(['a.test.ts:9.seed']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 1, evidence([run(3), run(9, { failed: true, casesRun: 2, counterexample: '[0]' })])))).toEqual(['exitCode', 'a.test.ts:9.failed']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3), run(9), run(5)])))).toEqual(['reports']);
    expect(fields(propertyOutcome(pin, sites, 0, evidence([run(3), run(9), { ...run(0), file: '' }])))).toEqual(['reports']);
  });

  test('no registration at all never holds on an empty report', () => {
    expect(propertyOutcome(pin, [], 0, evidence([run(3)])).held).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('propertyBaseCause', () => {
  const check = (onBase: 'fails' | 'passes'): PropertyCheckSpec => ({ id: 'p', kind: 'property', onBase, tests: ['a.test.ts'], ...pin, required: true, timeoutMs: 1000 });
  const result = (runs: PropertyRun[] | null, exitCode: number): CheckResult => {
    const property = runs === null ? null : evidence(runs);
    return { checkId: 'p', exitCode, stdout: '', stderr: '', suiteCount: 1, findings: null, property, expectation: propertyOutcome(pin, sites, exitCode, property), durationMs: 1, startedAt: '' };
  };

  test("'fails' is met only by a run that failed on the pinned seed", () => {
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('fails'), result([run(3, { failed: true, casesRun: 1, counterexample: '[0]' }), run(9)], 1))).toBeUndefined();
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('fails'), result([], 1))).toBe('no-result');
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('fails'), result(null, 1))).toBe('no-result');
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('fails'), result([run(3, { failed: true, seed: 1 })], 1))).toBe('no-result');
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('fails'), result([run(3), run(9)], 0))).toBe('contrary');
  });

  test("'passes' is met only when the comparison held", () => {
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('passes'), result([run(3), run(9)], 0))).toBeUndefined();
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('passes'), result([run(3), run(9, { casesRun: 1 })], 0))).toBe('suite-count');
    expect(propertyBaseCause(check('passes'), result([run(3), run(9, { failed: true })], 1))).toBe('contrary');
  });
});

describe('a property check in a manifest', () => {
  const base = { id: 'p', kind: 'property', onBase: 'fails', tests: ['a.test.ts'], seed: 42, numRuns: 25, required: true, timeoutMs: 1000 };
  const codes = (check: Record<string, unknown>): string[] => {
    const parsed = parseManifest({ checks: [check] });
    return parsed.ok ? [] : parsed.problems.map((p) => `${p.path}:${p.code}`);
  };

  test('is admitted with its tests, its declaration, and its pin', () => {
    expect(codes(base)).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('pins a 32-bit seed and a positive case count, with no default', () => {
    expect(codes({ ...base, seed: undefined })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].seed:not-integer');
    expect(codes({ ...base, seed: 2 ** 31 })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].seed:not-integer');
    expect(codes({ ...base, numRuns: 0 })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].numRuns:not-integer');
    expect(codes({ ...base, numRuns: 1.5 })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].numRuns:not-integer');
  });

  test('declares what it does on the base, names its tests, and pins no command', () => {
    expect(codes({ ...base, onBase: undefined })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].onBase:undeclared');
    expect(codes({ ...base, tests: [] })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].tests:missing');
    expect(codes({ ...base, command: ['node'] })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].command:not-applicable');
    // A property check may name a rule it states (A-R25-01), and like every check that does, it is required.
    expect(codes({ ...base, deniesRule: 'r' })).toEqual([]);
    expect(codes({ ...base, deniesRule: 'r', required: false })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].required:not-required');
  });

  test('no other kind pins a seed or a case count', () => {
    expect(codes({ id: 'a', kind: 'acceptance', onBase: 'fails', tests: ['a.test.ts'], seed: 1, required: true, timeoutMs: 1000 })).toContain('manifest.checks[0].seed:not-applicable');
  });
});

 /**
  * Reads a corpus and refuses it unless every file under it is pinned in its
  * manifest at the digest it holds, and nothing pinned is missing (I5).
  */
 export async function loadCorpus(version: string = CURRENT_CORPUS, root: string = CORPUS_ROOT): Promise<Corpus> {
   const dir = join(root, version);
   const manifest = parseManifest(await readFile(join(dir, MANIFEST), 'utf8'), version);
   const found = await digests(dir);
   const problems: string[] = [];
   for (const [path, digest] of Object.entries(found)) {
     const pinned = manifest.files[path];
     if (pinned === undefined) problems.push(`${path} is not pinned`);
     else if (pinned !== digest) problems.push(`${path} holds sha256:${digest}, pinned at sha256:${pinned}`);
   }
   for (const path of Object.keys(manifest.files)) if (found[path] === undefined) problems.push(`${path} is pinned and missing`);
   if (problems.length > 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version} does not match its manifest; a changed corpus is a new version: ${problems.join('; ')}`);
 
   const items: Item[] = [];
   for (const path of Object.keys(found).filter((p) => p.startsWith('items/')).sort()) {
     items.push(parseItem(JSON.parse(await readFile(join(dir, path), 'utf8')) as unknown, path, manifest));
   }
   const ids = new Set<string>();
   for (const item of items) {
     if (ids.has(item.id)) throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version} holds item '${item.id}' twice`);
     ids.add(item.id);
   }
   const digest = corpusDigest(found, manifest);
   const released = RELEASED[version];
   if (released !== undefined && released !== digest) {
     throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version} was released at sha256:${released} and now digests to sha256:${digest}; a changed corpus is a new version`);
   }
-  return { version, digest, root: dir, repos: manifest.repos, groups: manifest.groups, items };
+  return { version, digest, root: dir, repos: manifest.repos, groups: manifest.groups, arms: manifest.arms ?? DEFAULT_ARMS, items };
 }

  * it measured, so a released version never changes: an edit is a new version,
  * and repinning an old one in place is refused here, not trusted to a comment.
  */

+  v2: 'd145e2f1c36891361f2c9fd7757447cf1e5a5f3ac3c0fe237dc2bd11a7041064',

 /** The directory a repository's files are read from. */
@@ -133,35 +162,37 @@ export function render(content: Content): string {
 /** Every file under `dir` but the manifest, by forward-slash relative path, with its SHA-256. */
 export async function digests(dir: string): Promise<Record<string, string>> {
   const out: Record<string, string> = {};
   const walk = async (at: string): Promise<void> => {
     for (const entry of await readdir(at, { withFileTypes: true })) {
       const full = join(at, entry.name);
       if (entry.isDirectory()) {
         // A repository's install is never part of the corpus; it is made from the pinned lockfile per run.
         if (entry.name === 'node_modules') continue;
         await walk(full);
       } else if (entry.isFile()) {
         const path = relative(dir, full).split(sep).join('/');
         if (path !== MANIFEST) out[path] = createHash('sha256').update(await readFile(full)).digest('hex');
       } else {
         throw new Error(`benchmark: ${full} is neither a file nor a directory; the corpus holds only files`);
       }
     }
   };
   await walk(dir);
   return out;
 }

 function parseManifest(text: string, version: string): Manifest {
   const parsed = JSON.parse(text) as unknown;
   if (!isRecord(parsed) || parsed.version !== version || !isRecord(parsed.repos) || !isRecord(parsed.groups) || !isRecord(parsed.files)) {
     throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version}'s manifest must be { version: '${version}', repos, groups, files }`);
   }
   const repos: Record<string, RepoPolicy> = {};
   for (const [name, policy] of Object.entries(parsed.repos)) {
     if (!isRecord(policy) || !isStringList(policy.builderGlobs) || policy.builderGlobs.length === 0) {
       throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version}'s repo '${name}' needs builderGlobs, a non-empty list of globs`);
     }
     repos[name] = { builderGlobs: policy.builderGlobs };
   }

   for (const [name, prefixes] of Object.entries(parsed.groups)) {
     if (!isStringList(prefixes) || prefixes.length === 0 || prefixes.some((p) => p === '')) {
       throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version}'s group '${name}' needs its detector: a non-empty list of cause prefixes`);
     }
     groups[name] = prefixes;
   }
+  let arms: Record<string, Arm> | undefined;
+  if (parsed.arms !== undefined) {
+    if (!isRecord(parsed.arms) || Object.keys(parsed.arms).length === 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version}'s arms must name at least one arm`);
+    arms = {};
+    for (const [name, arm] of Object.entries(parsed.arms)) {
+      const overlay = isRecord(arm) ? arm.overlay : undefined;
+      if (!isRecord(arm) || typeof arm.verificationManifest !== 'string' || !isTreePath(arm.verificationManifest) || !isStringList(arm.acceptanceTests) || arm.acceptanceTests.length === 0 || !arm.acceptanceTests.every(isTreePath)
+        || !(overlay === null || (typeof overlay === 'string' && isTreePath(overlay) && overlay.startsWith('arms/')))) {
+        throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version}'s arm '${name}' needs a verificationManifest, a non-empty list of acceptanceTests, each a path inside the repository, and an overlay under arms/ or null`);
+      }
+      arms[name] = { verificationManifest: arm.verificationManifest, acceptanceTests: arm.acceptanceTests, overlay };
+    }
+  }
   const files: Record<string, string> = {};
   for (const [path, digest] of Object.entries(parsed.files)) {
     if (typeof digest !== 'string' || !/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(digest)) throw new Error(`benchmark: corpus ${version} pins ${path} at something that is not a SHA-256`);
     files[path] = digest;
   }
-  return { version, repos, groups, files };
+  return { version, repos, groups, ...(arms === undefined ? {} : { arms }), files };
 }

 ## What you write
 
 - Test files under the `testDirectory` that `run-requirements.json` names,
   each written for its `framework` and calling only the `publicInterface` it
   describes. Each test checks behavior the spec states.
 - `acceptance.json`, in the directory under `.drafts/`: `{ "checks": [...] }`,
   one check per test. An `acceptance` check names its test files in `tests`;
-  a `behavioral` check runs a pinned `command` that names its test file. Each check declares
+  a `behavioral` check runs a pinned `command` that names its test file. Where
+  the spec states what must hold for every input in a domain, and
+  it says the repository declares `fast-check` beside vitest, you may
+  also write a `property` check. It names its test files in `tests`, each
+  calling `fc.assert` on at least one property, and pins an integer `seed`
+  and a `numRuns`, the cases every property must run. Do not set either one
+  inside the file: the runtime sets both. Each check declares
   `onBase`: `"fails"` where the behavior is absent before the change, which is
   what reproduces it, and `"passes"` where the test guards something that
   holds already. At least one required check declares `"fails"`. For each
   authorization rule `security.json` names, write a test in which a caller the
   rule refuses is refused, and name the rule in that check's `deniesRule`.
 - `observation.json`, in the same directory, when the run observes its
   change once merged: `{ "works": [...], "regressed": [...], "rollback":
   { "regressed": [...], "consecutive": n } }`. Each check is `{ "app",
   "name" }`: the slug of the app that runs it, such as `github-actions`, and
   the check's exact name. Name only checks the spec says the repository's CI
   runs. `works` holds the checks that pass when the change works,
   `regressed` the checks that fail when it has regressed, and `rollback`
   the indexes into `regressed` that, failing together on `consecutive`
   commits in a row, call for a rollback. A check the target never reports is
   recorded as missing, so do not invent one.
 
 ## What you must not do
 
 - Do not test anything the spec does not state.
 - Do not change the spec's files. Nothing outside the test directory and the
   manifest is read.
 - Do not try to reach the repository or the network.
 
 ## How you are judged

     runtime({
       id: 'I5.stale-commit-is-refused-under-contention',
       title:
         'concurrent commitRunState calls from separate processes against one ifVersion leave exactly one winner holding the whole record; every loser is refused with a reason rather than silently applied, and a stale commit afterwards stores nothing',
       run: async () => {
         await withVaultDirs('p1-i5-contend-', async (dirs, base) => {
           const runId = 'i5-contend';
           const outcomes = await contendOnCommit(dirs, base, runId, '0', CONTENDERS);
 
           const winners = outcomes.filter((o) => o.ok);
           if (winners.length !== 1) {
             const detail = outcomes.map((o) => `${o.tag}: ${o.ok ? `stored ${String(o.version)}` : `refused (${o.message})`}`);
             throw new Error(
               `I5: ${String(CONTENDERS)} processes committed run state from version 0 and ${String(winners.length)} succeeded; ` +
                 'exactly one may. A lost update is a silent degrade of the only record task status lives in.' +
                 `\n  ${detail.join('\n  ')}`,
             );
           }
           const [winner] = winners;
           if (winner === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the winner list has one entry and no entry in it');
           if (winner.version !== '1') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the winning commit stored version ${String(winner.version)}, expected 1`);
           }
           for (const loser of outcomes.filter((o) => !o.ok)) {
             if (loser.message === '') {
               throw new Error(`I5: the refused commit from ${loser.tag} carries no reason; a refusal that says nothing is a silent failure`);
             }
           }
 
           // The record that landed must be one writer's whole state, not a
           // blend: each child tags its own `tasks`, so the stored state names
           // exactly one of them.
           const { LocalVault } = await import('@olympus-ai/vault');
           const vault = new LocalVault(dirs);
           const stored = await vault.readRunState(runId as RunId);
           if (stored.version !== '1') throw new Error(`I5: the stored state is at version ${stored.version}, expected 1`);
           const tags = Object.keys(stored.tasks);
           if (tags.length !== 1 || tags[0] !== winner.tag) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the stored state carries tasks [${tags.join(', ')}]; expected only the winner's ${winner.tag}`);
           }
 
           // The same refusal, uncontended: a commit from a version that has
           // already been superseded stores nothing at all.
           let stale = true;
           try {
             await vault.commitRunState({ ...stored, version: '0' }, '0');
           } catch {
             stale = false;
           }
           if (stale) throw new Error('I5: a commit from the superseded version 0 was accepted');
           const after = await vault.readRunState(runId as RunId);
           if (after.version !== '1' || Object.keys(after.tasks).join(',') !== winner.tag) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refused stale commit changed the store to version ${after.version} with tasks [${Object.keys(after.tasks).join(', ')}]`);
           }
         });
       },

     runtime({
       id: 'I5.over-request-refused',
       title:
         'the real engine refuses a level above the global cap, the station cap, or the role ceiling with reason exceeds-cap, carries no level on the refusal, and grants a request at or below the tightest bound at exactly the level asked for',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         // Three bounds, three different values, so each refusal names the one
         // that actually bit. `build` is capped below the role ceiling, which is
         // itself below the global cap.
         const overRequests: Array<{ readonly level: AutonomyLevel; readonly station: StationId; readonly bound: string }> = [
           { level: 2, station: 'build', bound: `the ${String(BUILD_CAP)} cap on build` },
           { level: 3, station: 'verify', bound: `the role ceiling of ${String(ROLE_CEILING)}` },
         ];
         for (const { level, station, bound } of overRequests) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, station, GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: L${String(level)} at '${station}' was granted at L${String(outcome.level)} against ${bound}. ` +
                 'An over-request must be refused; a downgrade returns a level the caller never asked for and did not ' +
                 'agree to, while reporting success.',
             );
           }
           if (outcome.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at '${station}' was refused as '${outcome.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
           }
           if (Object.hasOwn(outcome, 'level')) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries a level; there must be nothing on it to mistake for a grant`);
           }
           if (outcome.detail === '') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries no detail`);
           }
         }
 
         // The global cap, which the fixture document sets to the maximum and
         // so can never bite on its own. Lowered here, it must refuse a request
         // the station cap and role ceiling would both have allowed.
         const grounded = engine.resolvePolicy({ ...grantingDocument(), globalCap: 0, stationCaps: {} });
         const global = engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded);
         if (global.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I5: L1 at 'verify' was granted at L${String(global.level)} under a global cap of L0`);
         }
         if (global.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
           throw new Error(`I5: a request above the global cap was refused as '${global.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
         }
         if (!global.detail.includes('global L0')) {
           throw new Error(`I5: the refusal does not name the bound that bit: ${global.detail}`);
         }
         if (!engine.resolveAutonomy(0, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded).ok) {
           throw new Error('I5: L0 under a global cap of L0 was refused; the cap is a ceiling, not an exclusion');
         }
 
         // The control: a request within every bound is granted, and granted at
         // the level requested rather than raised or lowered to a cap. An engine
         // that refused everything would satisfy the loop above.
         for (const level of [0, 1] as const) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'build', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (!outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' is within every bound and was refused (${outcome.reason}: ${outcome.detail})`);
           }
           if (outcome.level !== level) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' resolved to L${String(outcome.level)}; a request inside the caps is granted as asked, never adjusted`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-document-refused-at-resolve',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy refuses a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument, even though the parameter type says it is one, so a caller that asserts past the compiler cannot resolve a grant out of a malformed document; a genuine document still resolves',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Each of these is what a caller produces by writing `value as
         // PolicyDocument`. Without the guard, the first resolves a global cap
         // of 99 and grants every level; the rest widen a scope the document
         // never authorised.
         const rogues: Array<{ readonly what: string; readonly value: unknown }> = [
           { what: 'a global cap of 99', value: { ...document, globalCap: 99 } },
           { what: 'a role ceiling of 99', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, autonomyCeiling: 99 } } } },
           { what: 'a station id that is not one of the ten', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, stations: ['deploy'] } } } },
           { what: 'an unknown top-level key', value: { ...document, backdoor: true } },
         ];
         for (const { what, value } of rogues) {
           let refused = false;
           try {
             engine.resolvePolicy(value as never);
           } catch {
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: resolvePolicy accepted ${what}. The parameter type is a compiler claim, and a control that holds ` +
                 'only while every caller remembers to validate first is not closed.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a genuine document resolves. A guard that threw on
         // everything would satisfy the loop above and break the engine.
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
         if (policy.globalCap !== document.globalCap) {
           throw new Error(`I5: a valid document resolved to globalCap ${String(policy.globalCap)}, expected ${String(document.globalCap)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-autonomy-level-refused',
       title:
         'resolveAutonomy refuses a requested level that is not one of the four, rather than comparing it against the cap: NaN, a negative, and a fraction all survive a numeric comparison and would otherwise be answered with success carrying a level that is not a level',
       run: async () => {
         const { AUTONOMY_LEVELS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         for (const [label, level] of [
           ['NaN', Number.NaN], ['-1', -1], ['2.5', 2.5], ['4', 4],

           let refused = false;
           try {
             const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level as never, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
             if (outcome.ok) {
               throw new Error(
                 `I5: resolveAutonomy answered ${label} with success at level ${String(outcome.level)}. ` +
                   'An unsupported input is a refusal, and success is the one answer a caller must never get for a ' +
                   'level nobody can honour.',
               );
             }
           } catch (error) {
             if (error instanceof Error && error.message.startsWith('I5:')) throw error;
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) throw new Error(`I5: resolveAutonomy returned a refusal rather than refusing ${label} outright`);
         }
 
         // The control: every real level is still answered normally.
         for (const level of AUTONOMY_LEVELS) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           const allowed = level <= ROLE_CEILING;
           if (outcome.ok !== allowed) {
             throw new Error(`I5: level ${String(level)} at 'verify' was ${outcome.ok ? 'granted' : 'refused'}, expected the opposite`);
           }
         }
