     runtime({
       id: 'I5.sandbox-egress-allowlist-enforced',
       title:
         'a sandbox provisioned with egress mode allowlist reaches the one host it names and no other, by name and by address; ' +
         'the direct route does not exist, so unsetting every proxy variable reaches nothing; an empty allow list is refused rather ' +
         'than read as deny-all or allow-all; and deny-all is unchanged, with --network none, no proxy, and no network created',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i5-egress-allowlist-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           // An allowlist that names nothing is refused. It is not deny-all wearing the wrong
           // name, and it is not permission to reach everything; either reading would be the
           // silent degrade this invariant exists to refuse (D-P2-07, reversed by P10).
           const empty = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [] } })));
           if (empty.layer !== 'egress') {
             throw new Error(`I5: an empty allowlist was refused at the ${empty.layer} layer, not the egress layer`);
           }
 
           // deny-all is untouched by the allowlist existing: no proxy is started beside a
           // sandbox entitled to no egress, and no network is created for it.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs), async (handle) => {
             const controls = provider.appliedControls(handle);
             if (controls.egress.mode !== 'deny-all') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all spec was applied as ${controls.egress.mode}`);
             }
             // `network: 'none'` is the type's guarantee once the mode is deny-all, so what is
             // read here is what Docker was actually told and what the container actually got.
             if (!controls.runArgs.join(' ').includes('--network none')) {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox was not started with --network none: ${controls.runArgs.join(' ')}`);
             }
             const interfaces = await provider.exec(handle, ['ls', '/sys/class/net']);
             if (interfaces.stdout.trim().split(/\s+/).join(',') !== 'lo') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox has interfaces [${interfaces.stdout.trim()}]; expected loopback alone`);
             }
           });
 
           const name = originName();
           const handle = await provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [name] } }));
           const { egress } = allowlistControls(provider, handle);
           try {
             await withOrigin(egress.proxy.outboundNetwork, name, async (origin) => {
               // The grant. The body proves the origin answered, not the proxy on its behalf.
               const reached = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c', `wget -T 8 -O - http://${origin.name}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (!reached.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was not reached: ${reached.stdout}${reached.stderr}`);
               }
 
               // Refused by name, and by address. The second is the one that matters: a refusal
               // that only ever fires on a name is a DNS failure wearing an allowlist's clothes.
               for (const host of [BLOCKED_NAME, BLOCKED_ADDRESS]) {
                 const answer = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${host}/ HTTP/1.0`);
                 if (!answer.includes('403') || !answer.includes(host)) {
                   throw new Error(`I5: ${host} is not on the allowlist and was not refused by name in the answer: ${answer}`);
                 }
               }
 
               // The strongest form: the origin's own address, which the proxy demonstrably
               // reaches, refused for the one reason under test — the address is not on the list.
               const sameHost = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ HTTP/1.0`);
               if (!sameHost.includes('403') || sameHost.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: a reachable host was allowed by address although only its name was granted: ${sameHost}`);
               }
 
               // There is nothing to bypass. The internal network carries no default route, so a
               // process that unsets every proxy variable is left with no second path to take.
               const routes = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'ip route']);
               if (routes.stdout.includes('default')) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the sandbox has a default route, so the proxy is a convention rather than the only way out: ${routes.stdout}`);
               }
               const bypass = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c',
                 'unset http_proxy https_proxy HTTP_PROXY HTTPS_PROXY no_proxy NO_PROXY; ' +
                   `wget -T 4 -O - http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (bypass.exitCode === 0 || bypass.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was reached with every proxy variable unset: ${bypass.stdout}`);
               }
             });
           } finally {
             await provider.destroy(handle);
           }
 
           // The proxy is the sandbox's, and goes with it. One left running is a route off the
           // host with no workload behind it and nobody watching it.
           for (const [kind, leaked] of [
             ['container', egress.proxy.name],
             ['network', egress.proxy.internalNetwork],
             ['network', egress.proxy.outboundNetwork],
           ] as const) {
             if (await dockerHas(kind, leaked)) throw new Error(`I5: the ${kind} ${leaked} outlived the sandbox it served`);
           }
         });
       },

     // R36a, extended by R36b: init's output, nothing written by hand, through the real driver to a merged pull request. Paid.
     external({
       id: 'I5.init-setup-merges-on-the-real-driver',
       title:
         "init's output on a TypeScript repository with vitest, with no file written by hand, drafting the spec and the tests from the README's intent, runs on the composed host with the Claude Code driver in every seat through each gate a person holds at L1 — the drafted spec, the drafted tests, and the merge — to a pull request merged into the base branch; paid (R36a, R36b)",
       level: 'runtime',
       package: '@olympus-ai/api',
       file: 'test/paid/init.paid.test.ts',
     }),
     // R15: no default declaration, nothing to reproduce is refused, and a check that did not start is not a failure.
     BASE_DECLARATION_HAS_NO_DEFAULT,
     NOTHING_FAILING_ON_THE_BASE_IS_REFUSED,
     UNSTARTED_BASE_CHECK_REFUSES,
     SUITE_CHECK_RUNS_WHAT_IT_NAMES,
   ],
   pending: [],
 };

===== packages/conformance/src/registry/property.ts =====
/**
 * R25: property checks. A property check runs locked property files under
 * the base's test framework, with the runtime's preload ahead of it, which
 * pins the seed and the case count and reports every run (D-R25-02).
 *
 * The rig is the hello fixture with a stand-in vitest, which imports the
 * files it is given, and a stand-in fast-check. The stand-in implements the
 * surface the preload uses: `configureGlobal`, `assert`, `property`,
 * `integer`, `stringify`, and `defaultReportMessage`. Its generator is seeded
 * and deterministic. The preload, the adapter that writes it, its reports,
 * and the comparison are the real ones. The stub sandbox runs on the host and
 * mounts nothing, so the rig maps the preload's mount onto its source. The
 * real fast-check under the real vitest, in the check image, is what the
 * benchmark's deterministic tier runs (D-R25-04).
 */
import { mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import type { Driver, TaskRequest, TaskResult } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { CheckResult } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import type { SandboxHandle, SandboxProvider, SandboxSpec } from '@olympus-ai/sandbox';
import type { BaseRunRecord, EvidenceBundle } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { runtime } from '../kit/assert.js';
import type { LocalAssertion } from '../kit/types.js';
import { ONE_RULE } from './decisions.js';
import { ARTIFACTS, HELLO_TASK, linePolicy, readRecord, refusalOf, stubDriver, withLine, writeManifest, type LineRig } from './line.js';

const api = async () => import('@olympus-ai/api');

const COMPILES = { id: 'hello-exit-zero', kind: 'compile', command: ['node', '-e', 'process.exit(0)'], required: true, timeoutMs: 10_000 };

/** The stand-in vitest: imports each file it is given after `run`, and exits 1 if any throws or none is given. */
const RUNNER = [
  "import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';",
  "const files = process.argv.slice(process.argv.indexOf('run') + 1);",
  'if (files.length === 0) process.exit(1);',
  'for (const file of files) {',
  '  try { await import(pathToFileURL(file).href); } catch { process.exit(1); }',
  '}',
  'process.exit(0);',
  '',
].join('\n');

/** The stand-in fast-check, ESM, at the entry its package.json exports. */
const FAST_CHECK = [
  'let global = {};',
  'export function configureGlobal(parameters) { global = { ...parameters }; }',
  'export function integer() { return { generate: (next) => (next() % 2001) - 1000 }; }',
  'export function property(arbitrary, predicate) { return { arbitrary, predicate }; }',
  'export function stringify(value) { return JSON.stringify(value); }',
  'export function defaultReportMessage(d) { return d.failed ? `Property failed after ${d.numRuns} tests { seed: ${d.seed} } Counterexample: ${stringify(d.counterexample)}` : undefined; }',
  'export function assert(prop, parameters = {}) {',
  '  const p = { ...global, ...parameters };',
  '  const seed = p.seed ?? 0;',
  '  const numRuns = p.numRuns ?? 100;',
  '  let state = (seed >>> 0) || 1;',
  '  const next = () => { state = (Math.imul(state, 1103515245) + 12345) >>> 0; return state >>> 8; };',
  '  let details = { failed: false, numRuns: 0, seed, counterexample: null };',
  '  for (let i = 0; i < numRuns; i += 1) {',
  '    const value = prop.arbitrary.generate(next);',
  '    details.numRuns = i + 1;',
  '    let ok;',
  '    try { ok = prop.predicate(value) !== false; } catch { ok = false; }',
  '    if (!ok) { details = { ...details, failed: true, counterexample: [value] }; break; }',
  '  }',
  '  if (p.reporter !== undefined) return p.reporter(details);',
  '  const message = defaultReportMessage(details);',
  '  if (message !== undefined) throw new Error(message);',
  '}',
  'export default { configureGlobal, integer, property, stringify, defaultReportMessage, assert };',
  '',
].join('\n');

/** A property that fails until the build writes `hello.txt`, whose name it builds rather than spells (D-R15-03). */
const HELLO_PROPERTY = 'hello.property.test.mjs';
/** A property that holds on every tree. */
const HOLDS = 'holds.property.test.mjs';
/** A property that holds, run by a file that sets its own case count below the pin. */
const FEW = 'few.property.test.mjs';
/** A file that registers a property and never reaches it. */
const UNREACHED = 'unreached.property.test.mjs';
/** A file that imports the framework and registers nothing. */
const NONE = 'none.property.test.mjs';
/** A property that fails for some inputs on every tree, so its counterexample depends on the seed alone. */
const SOMETIMES = 'sometimes.property.test.mjs';
/** The multiply suite: an acceptance test on three inputs, and a property over all of them. */
const MULTIPLY_TEST = 'multiply.test.mjs';
const MULTIPLY_PROPERTY = 'multiply.property.test.mjs';
const MATH = 'math.mjs';

const PROPERTY_FILES: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {
  [HELLO_PROPERTY]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nimport { existsSync } from 'node:fs';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), () => existsSync(['hello', 'txt'].join('.'))));\n",
  [HOLDS]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), (n) => n === n));\n",
  [FEW]: "import * as fc from 'fast-check';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), (n) => n === n), { numRuns: 1 });\n",
  [UNREACHED]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nif (process.env.NEVER_SET_BY_ANYONE === 'yes') fc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), (n) => n === n));\n",
  [NONE]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nexport const unused = fc.integer();\n",
  [SOMETIMES]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), (n) => n % 5 !== 0));\n",
  [MULTIPLY_TEST]: "import { multiply } from './math.mjs';\nfor (const [a, b, p] of [[3, 4, 12], [7, 0, 0], [-2, -5, 10]]) if (multiply(a, b) !== p) throw new Error(`multiply(${a}, ${b}) is not ${p}`);\n",
  [MULTIPLY_PROPERTY]: "import fc from 'fast-check';\nimport { multiply } from './math.mjs';\nfc.assert(fc.property(fc.integer(), (a) => multiply(a, 1) === a));\n",
  [MATH]: "export function multiply() { throw new Error('multiply is not written'); }\n",
};

/** R7's `hard-coded-output`, as JavaScript: right on the acceptance test's three inputs, a sum elsewhere. */
const HARD_CODED = 'export function multiply(a, b) {\n  if (a === 3 && b === 4) return 12;\n  if (b === 0) return 0;\n  if (a === -2 && b === -5) return 10;\n  return a + b;\n}\n';
const HONEST = 'export function multiply(a, b) {\n  return a * b;\n}\n';

/** Every file above but the source the build writes, which no one locks. */
const ALL_FILES = Object.keys(PROPERTY_FILES).filter((name) => name !== MATH);
const PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS = { ...ARTIFACTS, acceptanceTests: [...ARTIFACTS.acceptanceTests, ...ALL_FILES] };

const property = (id: string, tests: readonly string[], onBase: 'fails' | 'passes', seed = 42, numRuns = 25) =>
  ({ id, kind: 'property', onBase, tests, seed, numRuns, required: true, timeoutMs: 10_000 });

/** The fixture as a vitest repository that declares fast-check 4, with both stand-ins installed and every file above written. */
async function withProperties(rig: LineRig, options: { fastCheck?: boolean } = {}): Promise<void> {
  const root = rig.dirs.artifacts;
  const declared = options.fastCheck !== false;
  await writeFile(join(root, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'hello', private: true, devDependencies: { vitest: '4.1.11', ...(declared ? { 'fast-check': '^4.3.0' } : {}) } }));
  await mkdir(join(root, 'node_modules', 'vitest'), { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(join(root, 'node_modules', 'vitest', 'vitest.mjs'), RUNNER);
  await mkdir(join(root, 'node_modules', 'fast-check', 'lib'), { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(join(root, 'node_modules', 'fast-check', 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'fast-check', version: '4.3.0', type: 'module', exports: { '.': { import: { default: './lib/fast-check.js' } } } }));
  await writeFile(join(root, 'node_modules', 'fast-check', 'lib', 'fast-check.js'), FAST_CHECK);
  const { hashedLockfile } = await import('@olympus-ai/adapters');
  await writeFile(join(root, 'package-lock.json'), hashedLockfile('hello', ['node_modules/fast-check', 'node_modules/vitest']));
  for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(PROPERTY_FILES)) await writeFile(join(root, name), text);
}

/**
 * The stub sandbox with the mount table's `others` honoured for the one thing
 * a property check needs: the stub runs on the host and mounts nothing, so a
 * `file:` URL to a mount's target in a command's environment is rewritten to
 * its source.
 */
async function mounting(): Promise<SandboxProvider> {
  const { StubSandboxProvider } = await import('@olympus-ai/sandbox');
  const inner = new StubSandboxProvider();
  const mounts = new Map<SandboxHandle, SandboxSpec['mounts']['others']>();
  return new Proxy(inner, {
    get(target, key, receiver) {
      if (key === 'provision') {
        return async (spec: SandboxSpec) => {
          const handle = await target.provision(spec);
          mounts.set(handle, spec.mounts.others);
          return handle;
        };
      }
      if (key === 'exec') {
        return async (h: SandboxHandle, cmd: string[], options?: Parameters<SandboxProvider['exec']>[2]) => {
          if (options?.env === undefined) return target.exec(h, cmd, options);
          const env: Record<string, string> = {};
          for (const [name, value] of Object.entries(options.env)) {
            let mapped = value;
            for (const m of mounts.get(h) ?? []) mapped = mapped.split(`file://${m.target}`).join(pathToFileURL(m.source).href);
            env[name] = mapped;
          }
          return target.exec(h, cmd, { ...options, env });
        };
      }
      return Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
    },
  });
}

/** The rig's stub builder, made to write `path` with `text` in its workspace before it does the rig's own work. */
async function writing(path: string, text: string, sandbox: SandboxProvider): Promise<Driver> {
  const driver = await stubDriver();
  return new Proxy(driver, {
    get(target, key, receiver) {
      if (key !== 'runTask') return Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
      return async (req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> => {
        if (req.role.role === 'builder') {
          const wrote = await sandbox.exec(req.sandbox, ['node', '-e', `require('node:fs').writeFileSync(${JSON.stringify(path)}, ${JSON.stringify(text)})`]);
          if (wrote.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`the builder's write did not land: ${wrote.stderr}`);
        }
        return target.runTask(req);
      };
    },
  });
}

async function admissionProblems(rig: LineRig, checks: readonly unknown[]): Promise<ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; code: string }>> {
  const { startRun } = await api();
  await writeManifest(rig.dirs, checks);
  const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ sandbox: await mounting() }), { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS }));
  return !outcome.ok && outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? outcome.problems : [];
}

/** The base record of a run that crossed or stopped at `test-design`, and the result of `checkId` in it. */
async function baseResult(rig: LineRig, components: Awaited<ReturnType<LineRig['components']>>, checkId: string): Promise<CheckResult | undefined> {
  const ref = await components.vault.readBaseRun(rig.runId, null);
  if (ref === null) throw new Error('R25: the run left no base record');
  return (await readRecord<BaseRunRecord>(components.vault, ref)).checks.find((c) => c.checkId === checkId);
}

export const PROPERTY_CHECK_NEEDS_THE_SLOT: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.property-check-needs-an-adapter-slot',
  title:
    "a manifest that pins a property check over a base whose adapter set has no property slot is refused at admission, naming the check's kind; the same check over a base that declares fast-check 4 beside vitest 4 is admitted and runs",
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r25-slot-', async (rig) => {
      await withProperties(rig, { fastCheck: false });
      const problems = await admissionProblems(rig, [COMPILES, property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
      if (!problems.some((p) => p.path === 'manifest.checks[1].kind' && p.code === 'unbound')) {
        throw new Error(`R25: a property check on a set with no slot was not refused at admission: ${JSON.stringify(problems)}`);
      }
    });
    await withLine('r25-slot-control-', async (rig) => {
      await withProperties(rig);
      const { startRun } = await api();
      await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
      const sandbox = await mounting();
      const components = await rig.components({ sandbox });
      const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS }));
      const refusal = refusalOf(outcome, 'R25 slot control');
      if (refusal.at !== 'integrate' || refusal.state?.tasks[HELLO_TASK] !== 'passed') {
        throw new Error(`R25 control: a property check on a set with the slot did not carry the run to integrate: stopped at ${refusal.at} with ${JSON.stringify(refusal.transition)}`);
      }
    });
  },
});

export const PROPERTY_FILE_MUST_REGISTER: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.property-file-must-register-a-property',
  title: 'a locked property file that registers no property with the framework is refused at admission, naming the file, before any check runs',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r25-none-', async (rig) => {
      await withProperties(rig);
      const problems = await admissionProblems(rig, [COMPILES, property('none', [NONE], 'passes'), property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
      if (!problems.some((p) => p.path === 'manifest.checks[1].tests[0]' && p.code === 'no-property')) {
        throw new Error(`R25: a property file that registers nothing was not refused at admission: ${JSON.stringify(problems)}`);
      }
      if (problems.some((p) => p.path.startsWith('manifest.checks[2]'))) throw new Error(`R25: a file that registers a property was refused beside one that does not: ${JSON.stringify(problems)}`);
    });
  },
});

export const PROPERTY_CHECK_RUNS_EVERY_PINNED_CASE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.property-check-runs-every-pinned-case',
  title:
    "a property check whose file sets fewer cases than the manifest pins, or never reaches its registered property, does not do what it declared and refuses the test-design exit; the runtime's comparison names the short count and the registration that never reported; the same check over a file that runs every pinned case crosses",
  run: async () => {
    const { startRun } = await api();
    for (const [file, field] of [[FEW, `${FEW}:2.casesRun`], [UNREACHED, `${UNREACHED}:2`]] as const) {
      await withLine('r25-cases-', async (rig) => {
        await withProperties(rig);
        await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('short', [file], 'passes'), property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
        const components = await rig.components({ sandbox: await mounting() });
        const refusal = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS })), `R25 ${file}`);
        if (refusal.at !== 'test-design' || refusal.transition.reason !== 'base-mismatch' || !refusal.transition.mismatched.some((m) => m.checkId === 'short' && m.cause === 'suite-count')) {
          throw new Error(`R25: ${file} was not refused at the test-design exit for running short: ${refusal.at} ${JSON.stringify(refusal.transition)}`);
        }
        const result = await baseResult(rig, components, 'short');
        const named = result?.expectation?.held === false && result.expectation.mismatches.some((m) => m.field === field);
        if (!named) throw new Error(`R25: the comparison for ${file} did not name ${field}: ${JSON.stringify(result?.expectation)}`);
      });
    }
    await withLine('r25-cases-control-', async (rig) => {
      await withProperties(rig);
      await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('holds', [HOLDS], 'passes'), property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
      const components = await rig.components({ sandbox: await mounting() });
      const refusal = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS })), 'R25 cases control');
      if (refusal.at !== 'integrate') throw new Error(`R25 control: a property file that runs every pinned case stopped at ${refusal.at}: ${JSON.stringify(refusal.transition)}`);
      const result = await baseResult(rig, components, 'holds');
      if (result?.property?.runs.length !== 1 || result.property.runs[0]?.casesRun !== 25) throw new Error(`R25 control: the evidence did not record 25 cases: ${JSON.stringify(result?.property)}`);
    });
  },
});

export const PROPERTY_SEED_REPRODUCES: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I2.property-seed-reproduces-the-verdict',
  title:
    "a property check's evidence records the seed the manifest pinned, the cases run, and the counterexample; two runs on the same seed reach the same verdict on the same counterexample, and a run on another seed records that seed",
  run: async () => {
    const { startRun } = await api();
    const evidence = async (prefix: string, seed: number) =>
      withLine(prefix, async (rig) => {
        await withProperties(rig);
        await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('sometimes', [SOMETIMES], 'fails', seed), property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
        const components = await rig.components({ sandbox: await mounting() });
        refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS })), `R25 seed ${String(seed)}`);
        const result = await baseResult(rig, components, 'sometimes');
        const run = result?.property?.runs[0];
        if (result?.property?.seed !== seed || run?.seed !== seed || !run.failed || run.counterexample === null) {
          throw new Error(`R25: seed ${String(seed)} left evidence ${JSON.stringify(result?.property)}`);
        }
        return { held: result.expectation?.held, run };
      });
    const first = await evidence('r25-seed-a-', 7);
    const again = await evidence('r25-seed-b-', 7);
    if (JSON.stringify(first) !== JSON.stringify(again)) throw new Error(`R25: the same seed reached ${JSON.stringify(first)} and then ${JSON.stringify(again)}`);
    const other = await evidence('r25-seed-c-', 8);
    if (other.run.seed !== 8) throw new Error('R25: a run on another seed did not record it');
  },
});

export const PROPERTY_EDIT_IS_LOCK_TAMPER: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I3.property-check-edited-after-test-design-is-lock-tamper',
  title: 'a builder that rewrites a locked property file so that it holds on any tree is refused as lock-tamper, and the rewrite never judges the change',
  run: async () => {
    const { startRun } = await api();
    await withLine('r25-tamper-', async (rig) => {
      await withProperties(rig);
      await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
      const sandbox = await mounting();
      const driver = await writing(HELLO_PROPERTY, PROPERTY_FILES[HOLDS] ?? '', sandbox);
      const components = await rig.components({ sandbox, driver, reviewer: driver });
      const refusal = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS })), 'R25 tamper');
      const tampered = refusal.transition.reason === 'lock-tamper' ? refusal.transition.tampered.map((t) => t.path) : [];
      if (!tampered.includes(HELLO_PROPERTY)) throw new Error(`R25: a rewritten property file was refused as ${JSON.stringify(refusal.transition)}, not lock-tamper naming it`);
      if ((refusal.state?.evidenceRefs.length ?? 0) !== 0) throw new Error('R25: the rewritten property file judged the change: evidence was written');
    });
  },
});

export const PROPERTY_CATCHES_HARD_CODED_OUTPUT: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.property-check-catches-hard-coded-output',
  title:
    "R7's hard-coded-output, a multiply right on the acceptance test's three inputs and a sum elsewhere, passes its acceptance check and fails the property check at verify, the comparison naming the failed property; an honest multiply passes both",
  run: async () => {
    const { startRun } = await api();
    const acceptance = { id: 'multiply', kind: 'acceptance', onBase: 'fails', tests: [MULTIPLY_TEST], required: true, timeoutMs: 10_000 };
    const run = async (prefix: string, text: string) =>
      withLine(prefix, async (rig) => {
        await withProperties(rig);
        await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, acceptance, property('multiply-property', [MULTIPLY_PROPERTY], 'fails')]);
        const sandbox = await mounting();
        const driver = await writing(MATH, text, sandbox);
        const components = await rig.components({ sandbox, driver, reviewer: driver });
        const refusal = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS })), prefix);
        const bundles = await Promise.all((refusal.state?.evidenceRefs ?? []).map((r) => readRecord<EvidenceBundle>(components.vault, r)));
        const last = bundles.at(-1);
        return { refusal, accepted: last?.checks.find((c) => c.checkId === 'multiply'), judged: last?.checks.find((c) => c.checkId === 'multiply-property') };
      });
    const hard = await run('r25-hard-', HARD_CODED);
    if (hard.accepted?.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`R25: the hard-coded multiply did not pass its acceptance check, so the item is not R7's miss: ${JSON.stringify({ accepted: hard.accepted, at: hard.refusal.at, transition: hard.refusal.transition })}`);
    const failed = hard.judged?.expectation?.held === false && hard.judged.expectation.mismatches.some((m) => m.field === `${MULTIPLY_PROPERTY}:3.failed`);
    if (!failed || hard.refusal.state?.tasks[HELLO_TASK] !== 'parked') {
      throw new Error(`R25: the property check did not catch the hard-coded multiply: ${JSON.stringify({ judged: hard.judged?.expectation, task: hard.refusal.state?.tasks[HELLO_TASK] })}`);
    }
    const honest = await run('r25-honest-', HONEST);
    if (honest.refusal.at !== 'integrate' || honest.judged?.expectation?.held !== true) {
      throw new Error(`R25 control: an honest multiply did not pass the property check: ${honest.refusal.at} ${JSON.stringify(honest.judged?.expectation)}`);
    }
  },
});

export const POLICY_REQUIRES_A_PROPERTY_PER_RULE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.policy-requires-a-property-check-per-authorization-rule',
  title:
    "under a policy that sets propertyPerAuthorizationRule, an authorization rule with its denial check and no required property check naming it refuses the test-design exit, naming the rule, before the base runs; a required property check naming it in deniesRule lets the run cross; a property check never stands in for the denial check",
  run: async () => {
    const { startRun } = await api();
    const policy = await linePolicy({}, { propertyPerAuthorizationRule: true });
    const denies = { id: 'stranger-refused', kind: 'behavioral', onBase: 'passes', deniesRule: 'owner-only', command: ['node', '-e', 'process.exit(0)'], required: true, timeoutMs: 10_000 };
    const states = { ...property('owner-property', [HOLDS], 'passes'), deniesRule: 'owner-only' };
    const attempt = async (prefix: string, checks: readonly unknown[]) =>
      withLine(prefix, async (rig) => {
        await withProperties(rig);
        await writeFile(join(rig.dirs.artifacts, 'security.json'), JSON.stringify(ONE_RULE));
        await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [COMPILES, property('hello-property', [HELLO_PROPERTY], 'fails'), ...checks]);
        const components = await rig.components({ sandbox: await mounting() });
        const refusal = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components, { artifacts: PROPERTY_ARTIFACTS, policy })), prefix);
        return { refusal, base: await components.vault.readBaseRun(rig.runId, null) };
      });
    const unstated = await attempt('r25-rule-', [denies]);
    if (unstated.refusal.at !== 'test-design' || unstated.refusal.transition.reason !== 'denial-check-missing' || unstated.refusal.transition.rules.join() !== 'owner-only' || !unstated.refusal.transition.message.includes('property')) {
      throw new Error(`R25: a rule with no property check under the policy was ${JSON.stringify(unstated.refusal.transition)} at ${unstated.refusal.at}`);
    }
    if (unstated.base !== null) throw new Error('R25: the base ran before the rule with no property check refused the exit');
    const alone = await attempt('r25-rule-alone-', [states]);
    if (alone.refusal.transition.reason !== 'denial-check-missing' || alone.refusal.transition.message.includes('property')) {
      throw new Error(`R25: a property check stood in for the denial check: ${JSON.stringify(alone.refusal.transition)}`);
    }
    const both = await attempt('r25-rule-control-', [denies, states]);
    if (both.refusal.at !== 'integrate') throw new Error(`R25 control: a rule with both checks stopped at ${both.refusal.at}: ${JSON.stringify(both.refusal.transition)}`);
  },
});

===== packages/core/src/policy/engine.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/core/src/policy/engine.ts b/packages/core/src/policy/engine.ts
index e77869f..4347a62 100644
--- a/packages/core/src/policy/engine.ts
+++ b/packages/core/src/policy/engine.ts
@@ -197,123 +197,124 @@ function effectiveCap(policy: Policy, station: StationId, scope: CapabilityScope
 export class StrictPolicyEngine implements PolicyEngine {
   /**
    * Runs once at load. The result is deeply frozen and shares no object with
    * the document it came from, so a caller holding either cannot alter what
    * the runtime hashes into the Vault.
    *
    * The document is validated here even though the parameter is already typed
    * `PolicyDocument` (D-P3-09). The type is a compiler claim, and a caller that
    * asserts past the compiler — `junk as PolicyDocument` — otherwise reaches
    * this function with `globalCap: 99`, a station id that is not one of the
    * ten, or a role scope of any shape at all, and every one of those flows
    * through resolution into a grant. I5 says fail closed, and a control that
    * only works when the caller remembers to compose two functions in the right
    * order is not closed. Re-validating a document that was already validated
    * costs one pass at load.
    */
   resolvePolicy(doc: PolicyDocument): Policy {
     const checked = validatePolicyDocument(doc);
     if (!checked.ok) {
       throw new Error(
         'policy: resolvePolicy was given a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument. '
         + 'The parameter type says it is one, so this value reached here through an assertion '
         + 'rather than through validatePolicyDocument. Refused rather than resolved:\n'
         + formatDefects(checked.defects),
       );
     }
     const valid = checked.document;
     return frozen({
       globalCap: valid.globalCap,
       stationCaps: frozen({ ...valid.stationCaps }),
       approvals: totalApprovals(valid.approvals),
       roles: cloneRoles(valid.roles),
       protectedPaths: frozenCopy(valid.protectedPaths),
       // On a null prototype, as the role map is: a class is read only as an own key (D-P3-11).
       sensitivePaths: frozen(Object.assign(Object.create(null) as Record<string, string[]>, Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(valid.sensitivePaths).map(([c, globs]) => [c, frozenCopy(globs)])))),
       triggers: cloneTriggers(valid.triggers),
       concurrency: frozen({ ...valid.concurrency }),
       installRegistries: frozenCopy(valid.installRegistries ?? []),
+      propertyPerAuthorizationRule: valid.propertyPerAuthorizationRule ?? false,
     });
   }
 
   resolveAutonomy(
     requested: AutonomyLevel, station: StationId, role: RoleId, policy: Policy,
   ): PolicyResolution {
     // The parameter type is erased at run time, and the cap arithmetic below is
     // a numeric comparison that a non-member silently survives: `NaN > cap` is
     // false, and so is `-1 > cap` and `2.5 > cap`, so each would be answered
     // `{ ok: true }` carrying a level that is not a level (D-P3-12). I5 says an
     // unsupported input is a refusal, and a success is the one answer a caller
     // must never receive for an input nobody can honour. `PolicyRefusal.reason`
     // has no arm for a malformed argument and widening it is a contract
     // amendment, so this throws, as `resolvePolicy` does for a malformed
     // document.
     if (!isAutonomyLevel(requested)) {
       throw new Error(
         `policy: resolveAutonomy was asked for autonomy level ${String(requested)}, which is not one of 0, 1, 2, 3. `
         + 'The parameter type says it is one, so this value arrived through an assertion. Refused rather than compared.',
       );
     }
     const scope = scopeFor(policy, role);
     if (scope === undefined) {
       return {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'capability-missing',
         detail: `the policy defines no role '${role}', so it grants nothing at any station`,
       };
     }
     if (!scope.stations.includes(station)) {
       return {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'station-forbidden',
         detail: `role '${role}' may act at ${scope.stations.join(', ')}; '${station}' is not among them`,
       };
     }
     const cap = effectiveCap(policy, station, scope);
     if (requested > cap) {
       return {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'exceeds-cap',
         detail:
           `L${String(requested)} requested for role '${role}' at '${station}', `
           + `effective cap L${String(cap)} (${capDetail(policy, station, scope)}). `
           + 'Refused, not downgraded.',
       };
     }
     // The requested level, never the cap: a request within bounds is granted
     // as asked, and one outside them was refused above.
     return { ok: true, level: requested };
   }
 
   resolveCapabilities(role: RoleId, station: StationId, policy: Policy): CapabilityResolution {
     const scope = scopeFor(policy, role);
     if (scope === undefined) {
       return {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'capability-missing',
         detail: `the policy defines no role '${role}', so no scope exists to grant`,
       };
     }
     if (!scope.stations.includes(station)) {
       return {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'station-forbidden',
         detail: `role '${role}' may act at ${scope.stations.join(', ')}; '${station}' is not among them`,
       };
     }
     // A fresh frozen copy: the caller receives exactly what policy grants and
     // holds no handle on the policy itself.
     return { ok: true, scope: cloneScope(scope) };
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * Checks every `tools` grant in the policy against the inventory of tools a
  * driver actually offers (D-P3-04).
  *
  * `inventory` is mandatory and has no default. A validator that can be called
  * without one, or whose empty case means allow-all, validates nothing, and
  * every caller would take that path: an empty inventory against a non-empty
  * grant list is a refusal. The other half of the gap — a driver that can
  * produce a real inventory, since `DriverCapabilities` holds feature flags and
  * no tool list — is owed to P5 as `I4.driver-tool-inventory-validated`.
  */

 /**
  * The only path from an unvalidated value to a `PolicyDocument`. Returns every
  * defect it finds; an `ok: true` result means nothing was wrong, not that
  * something was repaired.
  */
 export function validatePolicyDocument(input: unknown): DocumentValidation {
   const defects: PolicyDefect[] = [];
   const record = shape(input, '', keysOf(DOCUMENT_KEYS), defects);
   if (record === undefined) return { ok: false, defects };
 
   const globalCap = read<AutonomyLevel>(record, 'globalCap', '', 'an autonomy level 0-3', isAutonomyLevel, defects);
   const stationCaps = readPartialMap<StationId, AutonomyLevel>(
     record, 'stationCaps', '', 'a station id', isStationId, 'an autonomy level 0-3', isAutonomyLevel, defects,
   );
   const approvals = readPartialMap<ApprovalKey, ApprovalOutcome>(
     record, 'approvals', '', `a 'station:level' key`, isApprovalKey,
     `one of 'auto', 'human-required', 'blocked'`, isApprovalOutcome, defects,
   );
   const protectedPaths = readArray<string>(record, 'protectedPaths', '', 'a non-empty path or glob', isNonEmptyString, defects);
   // Absent from a document written before R10: no path is sensitive (A-R10-01).
   const sensitivePaths = Object.hasOwn(record, 'sensitivePaths') ? validateSensitivePaths(record.sensitivePaths, defects) : {};
   // Absent from a document written before R34: no install may reach anything (A-R34-01, I4). Each entry is one host, as an egress entry is.
   const installRegistries = Object.hasOwn(record, 'installRegistries')
     ? readArray<string>(record, 'installRegistries', '', 'one host: no wildcard, path, or whitespace', (v): v is string => isNonEmptyString(v) && isHostLiteral(v), defects)
     : [];
+  // Absent from a document written before R25: no rule needs a property check (D-R25-05).
+  const propertyPerAuthorizationRule = Object.hasOwn(record, 'propertyPerAuthorizationRule')
+    ? read<boolean>(record, 'propertyPerAuthorizationRule', '', 'true or false', (x): x is boolean => typeof x === 'boolean', defects)
+    : false;
   const roles = Object.hasOwn(record, 'roles')
     ? validateRoles(record.roles, 'roles', defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'roles')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'roles', problem: 'required key is missing; expected an object keyed by role id' });
   }
   const triggers = Object.hasOwn(record, 'triggers')
     ? validateTriggers(record.triggers, 'triggers', defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'triggers')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'triggers', problem: 'required key is missing; expected a trigger policy' });
   }
   const concurrencyRecord = Object.hasOwn(record, 'concurrency')
     ? shape(record.concurrency, 'concurrency', keysOf(CONCURRENCY_KEYS), defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'concurrency')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'concurrency', problem: 'required key is missing; expected { maxParallelTasks, maxConflictRetries }' });
   }
   const maxParallelTasks = concurrencyRecord === undefined
     ? undefined
     : read(concurrencyRecord, 'maxParallelTasks', 'concurrency', 'a positive integer', isPositiveInteger, defects);
   const maxConflictRetries = concurrencyRecord === undefined
     ? undefined
     : read(concurrencyRecord, 'maxConflictRetries', 'concurrency', 'a non-negative integer', isNonNegativeInteger, defects);
 
   if (
     globalCap === undefined || stationCaps === undefined || approvals === undefined
-    || protectedPaths === undefined || sensitivePaths === undefined || installRegistries === undefined || roles === undefined || triggers === undefined
+    || protectedPaths === undefined || sensitivePaths === undefined || installRegistries === undefined || propertyPerAuthorizationRule === undefined || roles === undefined || triggers === undefined
     || maxParallelTasks === undefined || maxConflictRetries === undefined
     || defects.length > 0
   ) {
     // A defect with no undefined field is still a refusal: `shape` reports an
     // unknown key without failing the fields around it.
     return { ok: false, defects };
   }
   // A grant names a class the policy declares; one it does not would mount nothing and read as a grant (I4).
   for (const [roleId, scope] of Object.entries(roles)) {
     scope.sensitiveGrants.forEach((grant, i) => {
       if (!Object.hasOwn(sensitivePaths, grant)) {
         defects.push({ path: `roles.${roleId}.sensitiveGrants[${String(i)}]`, problem: `'${grant}' is not a class sensitivePaths declares` });
       }
     });
   }
   if (defects.length > 0) return { ok: false, defects };
   return {
     ok: true,
     document: {
       globalCap, stationCaps, approvals, roles, protectedPaths, sensitivePaths, triggers,
       concurrency: { maxParallelTasks, maxConflictRetries },
       installRegistries,
+      propertyPerAuthorizationRule,
     },
   };
 }

 export interface CheckResult {

   stdout: string; stderr: string;
   suiteCount: number | null;
   /**
    * `null` for a check whose exit code is its whole result. A behavioral
    * check's exit code is the product's: it can be 0 while the output is
    * wrong, and non-zero because the scenario expects an error. So when this
    * is present it is the verdict, a gate fails the check when `held` is false,
    * and the exit code is evidence rather than a verdict (A-P8-01). Required:
    * omission is not representable.
    */
   expectation: ExpectationOutcome | null;
   /**

    * scanner's report, or `null` for every other kind and for a security check
    * whose report could not be read, which is never an empty list: a scanner
    * that reported nothing readable has not shown the tree is clean (I5).

+/** What a property check ran: the pin it ran under, and one entry per report (R25). */

 describe('the base record (D-A-BF-01)', () => {
-  const reproduced = { checkId: 'reproduces', exitCode: 1, stdout: '', stderr: '', suiteCount: 1, expectation: null, findings: null, durationMs: 1, startedAt: '2026-10-11T00:00:00.000Z' };
+  const reproduced = { checkId: 'reproduces', exitCode: 1, stdout: '', stderr: '', suiteCount: 1, expectation: null, findings: null, property: null, durationMs: 1, startedAt: '2026-10-11T00:00:00.000Z' };
   const record = {
     runId,
     baseTreeSha256: 'c'.repeat(64),
     checks: [reproduced],
     unstarted: [],
     collectedBy: 'runtime' as const,
     draft: null,
     install: null,
   };
 
   test('is absent until written, then read back by the reference it was written under', async () => {
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toBeNull();
     const ref = await vault.recordBaseRun(record);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toEqual(ref);
     expect(JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(ref)))).toEqual(record);
   });
 
   test('is written once: a second base for the run is refused and the first stands', async () => {
     const ref = await vault.recordBaseRun(record);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [{ ...reproduced, exitCode: 0 }] })).rejects.toThrow(/already has a base record/);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toEqual(ref);
   });
 
   test('refuses a record the runtime did not collect, and stores nothing', async () => {
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, collectedBy: 'agent' } as never)).rejects.toThrow(/collectedBy/);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toBeNull();
   });
 
   test('R34: stores a whole install, and refuses one that is not the runtime\'s or names no tree digest', async () => {
     const install = {
       stack: 'npm', lockfile: 'package-lock.json', image: `node@sha256:${'d'.repeat(64)}`, steps: [['npm', 'ci', '--ignore-scripts']],
       lockfileSha256: 'e'.repeat(64), baseLockfileSha256: null, lockfileDiff: { added: ['node_modules/a'], removed: [], changed: [] },
       installedTreeSha256: 'f'.repeat(64), collectedBy: 'runtime' as const,
     };
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: { ...install, collectedBy: 'agent' } } as never)).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: { ...install, installedTreeSha256: 'x' } })).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: undefined } as never)).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     // A-R29-02: the steps it ran, at least one and none empty.
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: { ...install, steps: [] } })).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: { ...install, steps: [['python', '-m', 'venv', '--copies', '.venv'], []] } })).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install: { ...install, invocation: ['npm', 'ci'], steps: undefined } } as never)).rejects.toThrow(/install/);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toBeNull();
     const ref = await vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, install });
     expect(ref.kind).toBe('base-run');
   });
 
   test('refuses a result whose suite count is absent, since the suite-count rule would read it as met (codex-obs), and stores nothing', async () => {
     const { suiteCount: _omitted, ...uncounted } = reproduced;
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [uncounted] } as never)).rejects.toThrow(/not results/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [{ ...reproduced, suiteCount: -1 }] })).rejects.toThrow(/not results/);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [{ ...reproduced, suiteCount: 1.5 }] })).rejects.toThrow(/not results/);
     // R17: a security check's findings are the base set, so one the runtime could not have written is refused.
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [{ ...reproduced, findings: [{ rule: 'r', location: { path: 'a', line: 1 }, fingerprint: 'not-a-digest' }] }] })).rejects.toThrow(/not results/);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toBeNull();
   });
 
   test('refuses an exit code that is not an integer: NaN is written as null and read back as a failure', async () => {
     for (const exitCode of [Number.NaN, Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY, 1.5]) {
       await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, checks: [{ ...reproduced, exitCode }] })).rejects.toThrow(/not results/);
     }
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toBeNull();
   });
 
   test('is written once per draft hash beside the admitted checks\' (R19), and a draft\'s record is not the run\'s', async () => {
     const draft = 'a'.repeat(64);
     const admitted = await vault.recordBaseRun(record);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, draft)).toBeNull();
     const drafted = await vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, draft });
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, draft)).toEqual(drafted);
     expect(await vault.readBaseRun(runId, null)).toEqual(admitted);
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, draft, checks: [{ ...reproduced, exitCode: 0 }] })).rejects.toThrow(/already has a base record for draft/);
     expect(JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(drafted)))).toEqual({ ...record, draft });
     await expect(vault.recordBaseRun({ ...record, draft: 'not-a-hash' })).rejects.toThrow(/draft hash/);
   });
 
   test('a reference whose hash is not the record\'s reads nothing', async () => {
     await vault.recordBaseRun(record);
     await expect(vault.read({ runId, kind: 'base-run', hash: 'd'.repeat(64) })).rejects.toThrow(/hashes to/);
   });
